## Task 3 (0b-9c, part 1): `TenantFleetRunner` — the one fleet iterator

**Why a class and not `tenants:run`.** Three defects rule `tenants:run` out (spec §4.3.3): child options must be forwarded as `--option=['k=v']` and a bare child flag errors; `tenants:run` **discards child exit statuses**; and the entrypoint additionally catches failures and keeps booting. A half-synced fleet would be logged as completed while the app started serving mixed authorization state. There is therefore **exactly one** runner, shared by `permissions:ensure-fleet` (this wave) and `permissions:sync-fleet` (wave 1), because the universe predicates, the failure semantics, the per-tenant marker shape and the exit contract are the parts a gate has to re-verify and they are written **once**.

**Gate r9 m-1 is applied here.** The start snapshot is `get(['id', 'slug'])`, **not** `pluck('id', 'slug')` — `pluck` collapses duplicate keys rather than preserving a match set, and it cannot represent a cross-column collision at all. `tenants.slug` **is** globally unique (`apps/api/database/migrations/2025_11_30_000001_create_tenants_table.php:19`), so the reachable ambiguity is a selector equal to tenant A's **UUID** and tenant B's **slug** — never two equal slugs, which the schema forbids.

**Files**

- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Domain/Enums/FleetOutcome.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/DTOs/FleetTenantOutcome.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/DTOs/FleetRunResult.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenantFleetRunner.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Tenant/TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest.php`

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant`, Stancl `tenancy()`, the tenant migration path `database/migrations/tenant`.
- Produces: `TenantFleetRunner::run(array $onlyTenants, string $markerPrefix, \Closure $perTenant): FleetRunResult`, where `$perTenant` is `\Closure(Tenant): FleetTenantOutcome`.
- The **command** — never the runner — renders the aggregate marker line and returns the process exit code.

**Contract, restated so a reviewer can check it against the code**

1. **One start read defines the run's UNIVERSE.** `$directoryAtStart = Tenant::query()->get(['id', 'slug'])`. That set is the run's clock: **no later read may add a tenant to it or remove one from it.** Two later reads exist and are deliberate (rev 2 states them, because rev 1's "nothing later re-reads it except step 4" was false of the code — plan gate r1 minor): (a) each **selected** tenant is re-hydrated with `Tenant::query()->find($id)` immediately before it is processed, because the snapshot carries only `id` and `slug` while `runOne()` needs the full model to resolve a database name — and a row that **vanished** between the snapshot and now is `FAILED` with `tenant_not_ready:directory_row_disappeared`, never silently dropped; (b) step 4's closing `pluck('id')`, a pure population gauge. Neither read can enlarge the universe: (a) only ever narrows it, and (b) is never iterated.
2. **Selector resolution (step 1a).** Empty `$onlyTenants` ⇒ the whole snapshot. Otherwise each selector is matched against the snapshot on `id` (exact UUID) **or** `slug` (exact, case-sensitive) — one lookup, the **union** of matches, not a sequence with a fallback. Exactly one match contributes that tenant. Duplicates **collapse** (a tenant's id and its slug in one invocation yield one tenant, processed once, counted once). Zero matches ⇒ `FAILED`, `reason=tenant_selector_unmatched`, named by the **literal selector string**. More than one ⇒ `FAILED`, `reason=tenant_selector_ambiguous`, with the matched ids listed. Either failure counts in `failed=` and makes the run exit non-zero, and per-selector isolation matches per-tenant isolation — every selector that *did* resolve is still processed.
3. **Readiness, per tenant, at the moment of use.** (a) the database **physically exists** — the same check `TenancyResolver::initializeIfProvisioned()` performs (`apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenancyResolver.php:90`); (b) after `tenancy()->initialize()`, the tenant's `migrations` table contains **every** file under `database/migrations/tenant`. A tenant failing either is **`FAILED` with `reason=tenant_not_ready`** (sub-reason `database_absent` or `migrations_behind`) and the run exits non-zero. It is *not* an informational skip: nothing observable to the runner distinguishes a tenant that is mid-provisioning from an established tenant whose rolling migration just failed, and the entrypoint makes the second case routine — `tenants:migrate-rolling --force` runs unconditionally and its failure is caught and logged (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:150-154`). A fleet run that cannot prove it synced a tenant must not report success for that tenant.
4. **Per-tenant failure isolation.** One tenant's `FAILED`/`BLOCKED` never stops the run.
5. **The closing population gauge.** `gauges.provisioned_during_run = |directory at end| \ |directory at start|`. It is a **pure population count** and asserts **nothing** about those tenants' catalogue state: the directory row is written *before* the database is created, migrated or initialised (`TenantProvisioningService`), so a row's appearance is equally consistent with a provisioning attempt that never reached its own sync. It exists so an operator reading the aggregate line can see the fleet grew mid-run.
6. **Exit rule.** Non-zero if any tenant or any selector is `BLOCKED` or `FAILED`.
7. **`--dry-run` is WRITE-free, not FAILURE-free.** It changes exactly one thing — what is written — and nothing else. Classification, per-tenant markers (`mode=DRY_RUN`), the aggregate line and the exit rule are identical to an applying run. That belongs to the **closure**, not the runner: the runner never writes anything itself.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the enum and the two DTOs.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums;

/** One tenant's (or one selector's) result inside a fleet run. */
enum FleetOutcome: string
{
    case Applied = 'APPLIED';
    case AlreadyCurrent = 'ALREADY_CURRENT';
    case Blocked = 'BLOCKED';
    case Failed = 'FAILED';

    public function isGreen(): bool
    {
        return $this === self::Applied || $this === self::AlreadyCurrent;
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs;

use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\FleetOutcome;

final readonly class FleetTenantOutcome
{
    public function __construct(
        public FleetOutcome $outcome,
        public string $reason,
        /** The tenant id, or — for a selector failure — the literal selector string. */
        public string $subject,
    ) {}

    public static function applied(string $subject, string $reason = 'applied'): self
    {
        return new self(FleetOutcome::Applied, $reason, $subject);
    }

    public static function alreadyCurrent(string $subject, string $reason = 'already_current'): self
    {
        return new self(FleetOutcome::AlreadyCurrent, $reason, $subject);
    }

    public static function blocked(string $subject, string $reason): self
    {
        return new self(FleetOutcome::Blocked, $reason, $subject);
    }

    public static function failed(string $subject, string $reason): self
    {
        return new self(FleetOutcome::Failed, $reason, $subject);
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs;

use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\FleetOutcome;

final readonly class FleetRunResult
{
    /** @param list<FleetTenantOutcome> $outcomes */
    public function __construct(
        public array $outcomes,
        public int $provisionedDuringRun,
    ) {}

    public function tenants(): int
    {
        return count($this->outcomes);
    }

    public function count(FleetOutcome $outcome): int
    {
        return count(array_filter($this->outcomes, static fn (FleetTenantOutcome $o): bool => $o->outcome === $outcome));
    }

    /** @return list<FleetTenantOutcome> */
    public function nonGreen(): array
    {
        return array_values(array_filter($this->outcomes, static fn (FleetTenantOutcome $o): bool => ! $o->outcome->isGreen()));
    }

    public function isGreen(): bool
    {
        return $this->nonGreen() === [];
    }

    /** The closing aggregate line, e.g. `PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-FLEET tenants=3 …`. */
    public function aggregateLine(string $markerPrefix): string
    {
        $line = sprintf(
            '%s-FLEET tenants=%d applied=%d already_current=%d blocked=%d failed=%d gauges.provisioned_during_run=%d',
            $markerPrefix,
            $this->tenants(),
            $this->count(FleetOutcome::Applied),
            $this->count(FleetOutcome::AlreadyCurrent),
            $this->count(FleetOutcome::Blocked),
            $this->count(FleetOutcome::Failed),
            $this->provisionedDuringRun,
        );

        foreach ($this->nonGreen() as $outcome) {
            $line .= sprintf(' [%s %s reason=%s]', $outcome->outcome->value, $outcome->subject, $outcome->reason);
        }

        return $line;
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 2 — the runner.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Tenant\Application\Services;

use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs\FleetRunResult;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs\FleetTenantOutcome;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant;
use Closure;
use Illuminate\Support\Collection;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\File;
use Throwable;

/**
 * The ONE fleet iterator (spec 4.3.3, rev-8 gate r7 M-1).
 *
 * `tenants:run` cannot be one: it forces the --option=['k=v'] encoding on every
 * child flag, and it DISCARDS child exit statuses, so a half-applied fleet
 * would be reported as clean. This class calls the per-tenant closure DIRECTLY
 * and therefore holds the real result object.
 *
 * Shared verbatim by permissions:ensure-fleet (wave 0b) and
 * permissions:sync-fleet (wave 1). Only the closure, the marker prefix and the
 * status file differ.
 */
final readonly class TenantFleetRunner
{
    /**
     * @param  list<string>                          $onlyTenants ids or slugs; empty = every directory row
     * @param  Closure(Tenant): FleetTenantOutcome   $perTenant
     */
    public function run(array $onlyTenants, string $markerPrefix, Closure $perTenant): FleetRunResult
    {
        // 1. Read the directory ONCE. This read is the run's clock.
        /** @var Collection<int, Tenant> $directoryAtStart */
        $directoryAtStart = Tenant::query()->get(['id', 'slug']);
        $startIds = $directoryAtStart->pluck('id')->all();

        // 1a. Selector resolution against the START snapshot.
        [$selected, $selectorOutcomes] = $this->resolveSelectors($directoryAtStart, $onlyTenants);

        $outcomes = $selectorOutcomes;

        foreach ($selected as $tenantId) {
            /** @var Tenant|null $tenant */
            $tenant = Tenant::query()->find($tenantId);
            if ($tenant === null) {
                // The row vanished between the snapshot and now. Fail closed:
                // the run cannot prove it processed this tenant.
                $outcomes[] = FleetTenantOutcome::failed($tenantId, 'tenant_not_ready:directory_row_disappeared');

                continue;
            }

            $outcomes[] = $this->runOne($tenant, $perTenant);
        }

        // 4. Closing directory read — a pure population gauge.
        $endIds = Tenant::query()->pluck('id')->all();
        $provisionedDuringRun = count(array_diff($endIds, $startIds));

        return new FleetRunResult(array_values($outcomes), $provisionedDuringRun);
    }

    /**
     * @param  Collection<int, Tenant>  $snapshot
     * @param  list<string>             $onlyTenants
     * @return array{0: list<string>, 1: list<FleetTenantOutcome>}
     */
    private function resolveSelectors(Collection $snapshot, array $onlyTenants): array
    {
        if ($onlyTenants === []) {
            return [$snapshot->pluck('id')->all(), []];
        }

        $selected = [];
        $failures = [];

        foreach (array_values(array_unique($onlyTenants)) as $selector) {
            // A value is offered to BOTH columns and the UNION of matches is the
            // selector's match set — never a sequence with a fallback, which
            // would silently pick the id match and hide the collision.
            $matches = $snapshot
                ->filter(static fn (Tenant $t): bool => $t->getAttribute('id') === $selector || $t->getAttribute('slug') === $selector)
                ->pluck('id')
                ->unique()
                ->values()
                ->all();

            if ($matches === []) {
                $failures[] = FleetTenantOutcome::failed($selector, 'tenant_selector_unmatched');

                continue;
            }

            if (count($matches) > 1) {
                $failures[] = FleetTenantOutcome::failed(
                    $selector,
                    'tenant_selector_ambiguous:'.implode('|', $matches),
                );

                continue;
            }

            // Duplicates COLLAPSE: a tenant named twice (by id and by slug) is
            // processed once and counted once, or applied= would disagree with
            // the fleet size.
            $selected[$matches[0]] = true;
        }

        return [array_keys($selected), $failures];
    }

    /** @param Closure(Tenant): FleetTenantOutcome $perTenant */
    private function runOne(Tenant $tenant, Closure $perTenant): FleetTenantOutcome
    {
        $tenantId = (string) $tenant->getAttribute('id');

        try {
            // (a) the database physically exists — the same check
            // TenancyResolver::initializeIfProvisioned() performs.
            if (! $tenant->database()->manager()->databaseExists($tenant->getDatabaseName())) {
                return FleetTenantOutcome::failed($tenantId, 'tenant_not_ready:database_absent');
            }

            tenancy()->initialize($tenant);

            // (b) migrations are current.
            $pending = $this->pendingTenantMigrations();
            if ($pending !== []) {
                return FleetTenantOutcome::failed(
                    $tenantId,
                    'tenant_not_ready:migrations_behind:'.count($pending),
                );
            }

            return $perTenant($tenant);
        } catch (Throwable $exception) {
            return FleetTenantOutcome::failed($tenantId, 'exception:'.$exception->getMessage());
        } finally {
            if (tenancy()->initialized) {
                tenancy()->end();
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Files under database/migrations/tenant that the CURRENT (initialized)
     * tenant database has not recorded.
     *
     * @return list<string>
     */
    private function pendingTenantMigrations(): array
    {
        $path = database_path('migrations/tenant');
        if (! File::isDirectory($path)) {
            return [];
        }

        $onDisk = [];
        foreach (File::files($path) as $file) {
            if ($file->getExtension() === 'php') {
                $onDisk[] = $file->getFilenameWithoutExtension();
            }
        }
        sort($onDisk);

        /** @var list<string> $ran */
        $ran = DB::table('migrations')->pluck('migration')->all();

        return array_values(array_diff($onDisk, $ran));
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 3 — the two helpers this test needs, added to `ProvisionsTenantDatabases` in full** (plan gate r1 **M0b-1**). Rev 1 called `provisionTenants()` and `dropTenantDatabase()` and left their implementation to prose; **neither exists.** The trait's actual API is `provisionTenantDatabase(Tenant): Tenant`, `provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema(Tenant): Tenant` and `withinTenantDatabase(Tenant, callable): mixed` (`apps/api/tests/Traits/ProvisionsTenantDatabases.php:35,86,130`). Both helpers are written **in terms of those**, in the trait's own style, and both arms (PG and SQLite) mirror what `provisionTenantDatabase()` already does. Append them to the trait:

~~~php
    /**
     * N tenants, each with a real per-tenant database carrying the full schema.
     *
     * TenantFleetRunner's readiness predicate reads the tenant's `migrations`
     * table, so an EMPTY database is not enough: provisionTenantDatabase() alone
     * would make every tenant `tenant_not_ready:migrations_behind` and the
     * selector cases could never reach their closure. Hence
     * provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema().
     *
     * @return list<Tenant>
     */
    protected function provisionTenants(int $count): array
    {
        $tenants = [];

        for ($i = 0; $i < $count; $i++) {
            $slug = 'fleet-'.strtolower(Str::random(12));
            $tenant = Tenant::create([
                'name' => 'Fleet '.$slug,
                'slug' => $slug,
                'status' => TenantStatus::Active,
                'plan' => SubscriptionPlan::Trial,
                'country_code' => 'FR',
                'currency_code' => 'EUR',
            ]);

            $tenants[] = $this->provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema($tenant);
        }

        return $tenants;
    }

    /**
     * Delete a tenant's DATABASE while LEAVING its directory row — the
     * "directory row with no database" case TenancyResolver probes for, and the
     * one TenantFleetRunner must report as FAILED rather than skip.
     *
     * The connection is purged first: a cached `tenant` connection to a database
     * that no longer exists makes the NEXT initialize() throw a driver error
     * instead of returning false from databaseExists().
     */
    protected function dropTenantDatabase(Tenant $tenant): void
    {
        if (tenancy()->initialized) {
            tenancy()->end();
        }
        DB::purge('tenant');

        if (DB::connection()->getDriverName() === 'pgsql') {
            $tenant->database()->manager()->deleteDatabase($tenant);

            return;
        }

        $path = database_path($tenant->getDatabaseName());
        if (is_file($path)) {
            unlink($path);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Make a provisioned tenant look MIGRATIONS-BEHIND without touching the
     * migration files: delete the last recorded row from its `migrations` table,
     * so exactly one on-disk file is unaccounted for.
     *
     * @return string the migration name removed, for the assertion message
     */
    protected function makeTenantMigrationsBehind(Tenant $tenant): string
    {
        return $this->withinTenantDatabase($tenant, static function (): string {
            /** @var object{id: int, migration: string}|null $last */
            $last = DB::table('migrations')->orderByDesc('id')->first();
            if ($last === null) {
                throw new RuntimeException('tenant has no migrations recorded; provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema did not run.');
            }
            DB::table('migrations')->where('id', $last->id)->delete();

            return (string) $last->migration;
        });
    }
~~~

      New imports the trait needs: `App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\TenantStatus`, `App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\SubscriptionPlan`, `Illuminate\Support\Str`, `RuntimeException`. **Check each against the trait's existing import list before adding it** (`DB` and `Tenant` are already there at `:11,10`), and name the three additions in the handback per this plan's File Structure note.

      > **On the SQLite arm, `provisionTenantDatabaseWithSchema()` clones DDL from `sqlite_master` and copies no rows** (`ProvisionsTenantDatabases.php:86-117`), so the cloned `migrations` table is **empty**. That is fine for `makeTenantMigrationsBehind()` only on the **PG** arm, where `MigrateDatabase` really runs and really records rows (`:90-94`). `TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest` is a **PG-only** test for exactly this reason — see Step 5.

- [ ] **Step 4 — the selector test (EC-39), parameterised over the fleet commands.** In wave 0b only `permissions:ensure-fleet` exists; wave 1 extends the same class to `permissions:sync-fleet`. **The data provider is written now, with one real row, so wave 1 adds a row rather than a file** (plan gate r1 **M0b-1**; wave 1's plan promises to "extend" it). The provider's row is *(command name, the minimum arguments that make it runnable, the marker prefix it renders)*, and the command-level case asserts the two properties a fleet command must share regardless of what its closure does: **an unmatched selector exits non-zero**, and **the aggregate line names the selector**. The runner-level cases stay direct instantiations, because the selector contract is the runner's.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Tenant;

use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs\FleetTenantOutcome;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\Services\TenantFleetRunner;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\DataProvider;
use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\TestCase;
use Tests\Traits\ProvisionsTenantDatabases;

/**
 * EC-39 — the step-1a selector contract (spec 4.3.3's rev-9 M-2 ruling).
 *
 * The contrast that motivates it: RollingTenantMigrationCommand accepts a
 * single --tenant UUID, resolves it with an exact where('id', …), only WARNS
 * when it matches nothing, and then treats an empty tenant set as SUCCESS
 * (apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Commands/RollingTenantMigrationCommand.php:143-146,61-70).
 * Copying that shape would make a TYPO in the remediation command this
 * programme prescribes attempt zero tenants, print "no tenants", exit 0, and
 * let the operator write permissions_ensure=ok over a fleet never repaired.
 *
 * GATE r9 m-1 IS APPLIED: the ambiguous case is a CROSS-COLUMN collision — a
 * selector equal to tenant A's UUID and tenant B's slug — never two equal
 * slugs, because tenants.slug is globally unique
 * (apps/api/database/migrations/2025_11_30_000001_create_tenants_table.php:19).
 * The snapshot primitive is get(['id','slug']); pluck('id','slug') would
 * collapse duplicate keys and could not represent a match set at all.
 */
final class TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest extends TestCase
{
    use ProvisionsTenantDatabases;

    private TenantFleetRunner $runner;

    protected function setUp(): void
    {
        parent::setUp();
        $this->runner = new TenantFleetRunner;
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_empty_selector_list_is_the_whole_snapshot(): void
    {
        $tenants = $this->provisionTenants(2);

        $result = $this->runner->run([], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertSame(2, $result->tenants());
        $this->assertTrue($result->isGreen());
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_tenant_resolves_by_id_and_by_slug(): void
    {
        [$a, $b] = $this->provisionTenants(2);

        $byId = $this->runner->run([(string) $a->id], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));
        $bySlug = $this->runner->run([(string) $b->slug], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertSame([(string) $a->id], array_map(static fn (FleetTenantOutcome $o): string => $o->subject, $byId->outcomes));
        $this->assertSame([(string) $b->id], array_map(static fn (FleetTenantOutcome $o): string => $o->subject, $bySlug->outcomes));
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_id_and_the_slug_of_the_same_tenant_collapse_to_one(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $processed = 0;

        $result = $this->runner->run(
            [(string) $a->id, (string) $a->slug],
            'TEST',
            function (Tenant $t) use (&$processed): FleetTenantOutcome {
                $processed++;

                return FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id);
            },
        );

        $this->assertSame(1, $processed, 'duplication is an operator convenience, not an error — and double-processing would make applied= disagree with the fleet size.');
        $this->assertSame(1, $result->tenants());
        $this->assertTrue($result->isGreen());
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_unmatched_selector_fails_by_its_literal_string_and_does_not_abandon_the_rest(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $processed = [];

        $result = $this->runner->run(
            ['no-such-tenant', (string) $a->id],
            'TEST',
            function (Tenant $t) use (&$processed): FleetTenantOutcome {
                $processed[] = (string) $t->id;

                return FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id);
            },
        );

        $this->assertSame([(string) $a->id], $processed, 'per-selector isolation: one typo must not abandon the rest of a remediation batch.');
        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen());
        $line = $result->aggregateLine('TEST');
        $this->assertStringContainsString('failed=1', $line);
        $this->assertStringContainsString('no-such-tenant', $line);
        $this->assertStringContainsString('tenant_selector_unmatched', $line);
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_cross_column_collision_is_ambiguous_and_names_both_ids(): void
    {
        [$a, $b] = $this->provisionTenants(2);
        // The reachable ambiguity: tenant B's SLUG is set to tenant A's UUID.
        // slug is globally unique, so this is legal; two equal slugs are not.
        $b->forceFill(['slug' => (string) $a->id])->save();

        $result = $this->runner->run([(string) $a->id], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen());
        $line = $result->aggregateLine('TEST');
        $this->assertStringContainsString('tenant_selector_ambiguous', $line);
        $this->assertStringContainsString((string) $a->id, $line);
        $this->assertStringContainsString((string) $b->id, $line);
    }

    #[Test]
    public function an_unmatched_selector_does_not_exit_zero_the_way_rolling_migration_does(): void
    {
        $this->provisionTenants(1);

        $result = $this->runner->run(['typo-in-the-runbook'], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertSame(0, $result->count(\App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Enums\FleetOutcome::Applied));
        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen(), 'a zero-match run must NOT be green — that is the RollingTenantMigrationCommand shape this contract deliberately rejects.');
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_tenant_whose_database_is_absent_is_failed_not_skipped(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $this->dropTenantDatabase($a);

        $result = $this->runner->run([], 'TEST', fn (Tenant $t): FleetTenantOutcome => FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id));

        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen());
        $this->assertStringContainsString('tenant_not_ready:database_absent', $result->aggregateLine('TEST'));
    }

    /**
     * The OTHER half of the readiness predicate, and the one that actually
     * happens in production: the entrypoint runs `tenants:migrate-rolling
     * --force` unconditionally and CATCHES its failure
     * (apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:150-154), so an established tenant sitting
     * one migration behind is routine. Rev 1 of this plan tested only the absent
     * database and left this arm — half the predicate — unexercised
     * (plan gate r1 M0b-1).
     */
    #[Test]
    public function a_tenant_whose_migrations_are_behind_is_failed_not_skipped(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $removed = $this->makeTenantMigrationsBehind($a);
        $reached = false;

        $result = $this->runner->run([], 'TEST', function (Tenant $t) use (&$reached): FleetTenantOutcome {
            $reached = true;

            return FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id);
        });

        $this->assertFalse($reached, "the closure must not run for a tenant missing '{$removed}': a fleet run that cannot prove it synced a tenant must not report success for it.");
        $this->assertFalse($result->isGreen());
        $this->assertStringContainsString('tenant_not_ready:migrations_behind:1', $result->aggregateLine('TEST'));
    }

    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
    // THE TWO MEMBERS BELOW SHIP IN TASK 4'S COMMIT, NOT THIS ONE.
    // They invoke `permissions:ensure-fleet`, which Task 4 creates; committing
    // them here would be a knowingly red commit (Global Constraints, plan gate
    // r1 B0b-2). They are written out here because this is the class that owns
    // them and the class wave 1 extends. Task 4 Step 11 adds them and
    // re-stages this file.
    // ------------------------------------------------------------------

    /**
     * Wave 1 ADDS A ROW HERE for permissions:sync-fleet. The two properties are
     * the ones every fleet command shares no matter what its closure does, and
     * they are exactly the two RollingTenantMigrationCommand gets wrong.
     *
     * @return array<string, array{0: string, 1: array<string, mixed>, 2: string}>
     */
    public static function fleetCommands(): array
    {
        return [
            'permissions:ensure-fleet' => [
                'permissions:ensure-fleet',
                ['--keys' => ['products.view'], '--dry-run' => true],
                'PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-FLEET',
            ],
            // wave 1 appends:
            // 'permissions:sync-fleet' => ['permissions:sync-fleet', ['--dry-run' => true], 'PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET'],
        ];
    }

    /**
     * @param  array<string, mixed>  $arguments
     */
    #[Test]
    #[DataProvider('fleetCommands')]
    public function every_fleet_command_exits_non_zero_on_an_unmatched_selector(string $command, array $arguments, string $markerPrefix): void
    {
        $this->provisionTenants(1);

        $exitCode = $this->artisan($command, [...$arguments, '--tenants' => ['typo-in-the-runbook']])
            ->expectsOutputToContain('tenant_selector_unmatched')
            ->expectsOutputToContain($markerPrefix)
            ->run();

        $this->assertSame(
            1,
            $exitCode,
            "{$command} exited 0 on a selector that matched nothing. That is the RollingTenantMigrationCommand shape this contract rejects: an operator would write permissions_ensure=ok over a fleet never repaired.",
        );
    }

    #[Test]
    public function a_tenant_provisioned_after_the_start_snapshot_is_counted_as_a_population_gauge_only(): void
    {
        [$a] = $this->provisionTenants(1);
        $late = null;

        $result = $this->runner->run([], 'TEST', function (Tenant $t) use (&$late): FleetTenantOutcome {
            $late ??= $this->provisionTenants(1)[0];

            return FleetTenantOutcome::applied((string) $t->id);
        });

        $this->assertSame(1, $result->tenants(), 'a directory row that appears after the snapshot is not in this run\'s universe.');
        $this->assertSame(1, $result->provisionedDuringRun);
        $this->assertTrue($result->isGreen(), 'the gauge asserts NOTHING about that tenant\'s catalogue state and must never make the run non-green.');
    }
}
~~~

## Task 4 (0b-9c part 2 + 0b-14): `permissions:ensure`, `permissions:ensure-fleet`, and the lock wired into every runtime writer — **ONE commit**

> **This is one commit by spec ruling, not by preference.** §8's 0b-14 row: *"WIRING that lock into every runtime role-grant writer that exists at 0b, **in the same lane and the same commit as `EnsurePermissions.php`**"*. The 0b-window argument (§4.3.2, rev-7 B-1) is why: with every runtime writer serialized on the one advisory key, `ensure`'s post-lock eligibility check is decisive on its own — any operator change to a `--grant-to` role either commits **before** `ensure` acquires the lock (the post-lock re-read finds the role no longer equal to `postA1aGrants`, `matchesPreWave0b()` is false, and the role falls into `admin_only=`) or commits **after** `ensure` releases (and lands on top of a grant set `ensure` had already decided was eligible). There is no third interleaving **because there is no unlocked writer left to produce one**. Split the two and there is: for the whole 0b window `ensure` would be serialized against nothing, and the equality check would be a race with no detector.

### Why `permissions:ensure` exists at all

After `lane/w-lot-a-1a` merges, `php artisan tenants:seed --class=…RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` **writes nothing** on a marked tenant while `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE` is false — its shipped default. `run()` falls past the enforcement branch, hits `markedTenantCarriesWlota1aDelta()`, emits its `WLOTA1A-RESEED … marked_tenant_delta_preserved` marker and **returns** (lane `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:52-55`, predicate at `:72-78`). That is *correct* for the lane, whose whole point is that a marked tenant is never re-seeded. The consequence for 0b is fatal: the nineteen keys would never reach a marked tenant while its routes are already gated on them, and Spatie fails closed — **403 for everyone, including `admin`**. That is the `credit-notes.cancel` failure mode reproduced on nineteen fresh keys.

### The contract, deliberately the smallest thing that closes the hole

- **Creates only, through an explicit existence check.** Never renames, never deprecates, never deletes, never revokes. The implementation is `exists()` **then** `Permission::create(...)` rather than `Permission::firstOrCreate(...)`, and rev 2 says so in both places (rev 1's prose said `firstOrCreate` while its code did `exists()`+`create()` — plan gate r1 minor). The reason is not style: the runner must **count** creations (`created=`) and must be able to count them in a `--dry-run` where nothing is written, and `firstOrCreate` gives neither — it returns a model without telling you which branch it took, and it writes on the dry-run path. Under the advisory lock the two-statement form has no race the one-statement form does not.
- **Grants to `admin` unconditionally — and a tenant with no resolvable `admin` is a FAILURE.** The registry-era rule is `admin = activeKeys()` and `admin` is not operator-editable at all, so granting `admin` a new key restores an invariant rather than widening a choice. **The converse is the hard part** (plan gate r1 **B0b-5**): if the `admin` role cannot be resolved, the tenant has **nobody** holding the keys its routes are now gated on — Spatie fails closed, so that tenant is 403 for every caller on twenty-five write routes. Rev 1 appended `'admin'` to `missingRoles`, continued, and computed the outcome from `created`/`granted` alone, so such a tenant could emit `APPLIED` or `ALREADY_CURRENT`, exit **zero**, and be marked deploy-safe. Rev 2: a missing or unresolvable `admin` is **`FAILED` with `reason=admin_role_missing`**, non-zero exit, on **both** the applying and the `--dry-run` path — a dry run is write-free, not failure-free.
- **Grants a named `--grant-to` role only when `matchesPreWave0b()` is true**, evaluated **once per role, from the post-lock re-read, before any of that role's additions are written**, and the approved additions are then written as **one batch** diffed against **that same snapshot**. Evaluating per key would be the same defect at a smaller scale: the first granted key makes the role unequal and every subsequent key in the same invocation would be refused. **One read, and the read is a value that is passed, not a call that is repeated** (plan gate r2 **M2-1**) — `grantMissing(Role $role, array $keys, array $current, bool $write)` takes the snapshot as a parameter, so "snapshot ONCE" is a property of the signature rather than a promise in a comment, and case 5 of `EnsurePermissionsCustomisationGuardTest` can count the reads.
- **Otherwise: `admin` only, and say so.** The role is skipped, listed in `admin_only=`, and printed with the operator sentence. **A skip is not a failure** — the run still exits 0, because a tenant that customised its `manager` and therefore does not receive a new key is the correct outcome, not an incident.
- **Writes no marker.** It touches neither `provisioning_source` nor `general_manager`'s existence, which is exactly why it is safe where `tenants:seed` deliberately no-ops.
- **Team-bounded and NULL-team-tolerant.** `SetPermissionsTeam` is HTTP-only (`apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Middleware/SetPermissionsTeam.php:22-29`), and a console command has nothing to supply the team from. `ensure` therefore takes the identical boundary the lane ships — set before the transaction, restored in a `finally` on **every** path including failure — and resolves every role with the lane's NULL-or-tenant predicate. After the lane merges the legacy catalogue roles stay **NULL-team** while `general_manager` is **tenant-team-scoped**, so a lookup that ignores the team column would either miss `general_manager` entirely or resolve a different row than the `FOR UPDATE` that locked it — and a lock over one topology guarding a write over another is not serialization.

### Steps — part A: the runner

- [ ] **Step 1 — the outcome enum and the result DTO.**

<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums;

~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs;

use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\EnsurePermissionsOutcome;

final readonly class EnsurePermissionsResult
{
    /**
     * @param  list<string>  $adminOnly     roles skipped because they are no longer pre-wave-0b pristine
     * @param  list<string>  $missingRoles  --grant-to names with no resolvable role in this tenant
     */
    public function __construct(
        public EnsurePermissionsOutcome $outcome,
        public string $reason,
        public int $created,
        public int $granted,
        public array $adminOnly,
        public array $missingRoles,
    ) {}

    /**
     * The per-tenant marker line, spec 4.3.5, same shape as the sync marker.
     *
     * `mode` is rendered HERE, by the DTO, and is therefore identical on the
     * single-tenant and the fleet path. Rev 1 had the fleet command append it
     * after `reason=` while the single-tenant command never added it at all, so
     * two commands with one contract produced two log shapes and only one of
     * them was greppable for `mode=DRY_RUN` (plan gate r1 minor).
     */
    public function markerLine(string $tenantId, bool $write): string
    {
        return sprintf(
            'PERMISSIONS-ENSURE tenant=%s outcome=%s mode=%s created=%d granted=%d admin_only=%s missing_roles=%s reason=%s',
            $tenantId,
            $this->outcome->value,
            $write ? 'APPLY' : 'DRY_RUN',
            $this->created,
            $this->granted,
            $this->adminOnly === [] ? '-' : implode(',', $this->adminOnly),
            $this->missingRoles === [] ? '-' : implode(',', $this->missingRoles),
            $this->reason,
        );
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 2 — the runner. This is the per-tenant routine both commands call.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\Services;

use App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs\EnsurePermissionsResult;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\LegacyRoleBaseline;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\EnsurePermissionsOutcome;
use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Locks\PermissionWriteLock;
use DomainException;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Collection;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB;
use Spatie\Permission\Models\Permission;
use Spatie\Permission\Models\Role;
use Spatie\Permission\PermissionRegistrar;
use Throwable;

/**
 * The wave-0b stopgap catalogue writer (spec 4.3.5).
 *
 * It exists because after lane/w-lot-a-1a merges, `tenants:seed
 * RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` writes NOTHING on a marked tenant while
 * LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE is false (its shipped default) — correct for
 * that lane, fatal for a wave that gates live routes on nineteen new keys.
 *
 * permissions:sync SUPERSEDES it entirely in wave 1 (step 2 creates every
 * registry key and step 6 applies template grants, a strict superset), which is
 * why wave 1 deletes this class, both commands and their deploy rows in the
 * same commit that lands permissions:sync.
 */
final readonly class EnsurePermissionsRunner
{
    public function __construct(
        private PermissionRegistrar $permissionRegistrar,
        private PermissionWriteLock $writeLock,
        private LegacyRoleBaseline $baseline,
    ) {}

    /**
     * @param  list<string>  $keys
     * @param  list<string>  $grantTo
     */
    public function run(string $tenantId, array $keys, array $grantTo, bool $write): EnsurePermissionsResult
    {
        // The team boundary lane/w-lot-a-1a ships, adopted verbatim: set before
        // the transaction, restored in a finally that runs on EVERY path.
        $previousTeamId = $this->permissionRegistrar->getPermissionsTeamId();
        $this->permissionRegistrar->setPermissionsTeamId($tenantId);

        try {
            return DB::transaction(function () use ($tenantId, $keys, $grantTo, $write): EnsurePermissionsResult {
                // Obligation (ii): the lock is the FIRST statement in the transaction.
                $this->writeLock->acquire($tenantId);

                $created = 0;
                $granted = 0;
                $adminOnly = [];
                $missingRoles = [];

                // Obligation (iii): FOR UPDATE the affected roles, ordered by
                // roles.name ASC then roles.id ASC — the lane's order.
                $wanted = array_values(array_unique(['admin', ...$grantTo]));
                $roles = $this->resolveCatalogueRoles($tenantId, $wanted);

                // 1. Create-only.
                foreach ($keys as $key) {
                    $exists = Permission::query()
                        ->where('name', $key)
                        ->where('guard_name', 'sanctum')
                        ->exists();
                    if ($exists) {
                        continue;
                    }
                    $created++;
                    if ($write) {
                        Permission::create(['name' => $key, 'guard_name' => 'sanctum']);
                    }
                }

                // 2. admin — unconditional. Restores an invariant, never widens a
                // choice. A tenant with NO resolvable admin is a FAILURE, not a
                // note: its routes are gated on keys nobody holds, and Spatie
                // fails closed, so every caller including the operator running
                // this command gets 403 on twenty-five write routes. Throwing
                // here also rolls the transaction back, which is correct — a run
                // that cannot reach admin has nothing it should have written.
                // (Plan gate r1 B0b-5.)
                $admin = $roles->get('admin');
                if ($admin === null) {
                    throw new AdminRoleMissing($tenantId);
                }
                // ONE read per role, here as everywhere: the snapshot is taken
                // and then HANDED to grantMissing(). Rev 2 read it a second time
                // inside grantMissing(), which contradicted the snapshot-ONCE
                // contract two lines below and made the one-read assertion in
                // EnsurePermissionsCustomisationGuardTest case 5 unsatisfiable
                // (plan gate r2 M2-1).
                $granted += $this->grantMissing($admin, $keys, $this->currentKeys($admin), $write);

                // 3. Every other --grant-to role, equality-guarded, snapshot ONCE.
                foreach ($grantTo as $roleName) {
                    if ($roleName === 'admin') {
                        continue;
                    }
                    $role = $roles->get($roleName);
                    if ($role === null) {
                        $missingRoles[] = $roleName;

                        continue;
                    }

                    // Obligation (iv): the eligibility decision is made from the
                    // POST-LOCK read, once per role, BEFORE any of that role's
                    // additions are written. Per-key evaluation would refuse every
                    // key after the first in the same invocation.
                    $current = $this->currentKeys($role);
                    if (! $this->baseline->matchesPreWave0b($roleName, $current)) {
                        $adminOnly[] = $roleName;

                        continue;
                    }

                    // $current — the SAME array the eligibility decision was made
                    // from — is what the batch is diffed against. Re-reading here
                    // would be a second read of the same rows inside the same
                    // transaction: harmless for correctness under the lock, but it
                    // would make the contract untestable, and a contract nothing
                    // can observe is a comment.
                    $granted += $this->grantMissing($role, $keys, $current, $write);
                }

                $outcome = ($created === 0 && $granted === 0)
                    ? EnsurePermissionsOutcome::AlreadyCurrent
                    : EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Applied;

                $result = new EnsurePermissionsResult(
                    $outcome,
                    $write ? 'ensure_applied' : 'dry_run',
                    $created,
                    $granted,
                    array_values(array_unique($adminOnly)),
                    array_values(array_unique($missingRoles)),
                );

                if (! $write) {
                    // Write-free, not failure-free: the classification above is
                    // identical to an applying run; only the rows are rolled back.
                    throw new DryRunRollback($result);
                }

                return $result;
            });
        } catch (DryRunRollback $rollback) {
            // A dry run is WRITE-free, not FAILURE-free: the sentinel carries the
            // classification out of the rolled-back transaction UNCHANGED, so an
            // admin_role_missing tenant is FAILED on the dry-run path too.
            return $rollback->result;
        } catch (AdminRoleMissing) {
            return new EnsurePermissionsResult(
                EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Failed,
                'admin_role_missing',
                0,
                0,
                [],
                ['admin'],
            );
        } catch (DomainException $exception) {
            return new EnsurePermissionsResult(
                EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Blocked,
                $exception->getMessage(),
                0,
                0,
                [],
                [],
            );
        } catch (Throwable $exception) {
            // EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Failed must be REACHABLE from the runner,
            // or the single-tenant command's promised FAILED marker is a shape no
            // code can produce and a non-DomainException escapes as an uncaught
            // stack trace with no marker at all (plan gate r1 minor). The message
            // is the reason token; the fleet command turns it into a
            // FleetTenantOutcome::failed and a non-zero exit.
            return new EnsurePermissionsResult(
                EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Failed,
                'exception:'.$exception->getMessage(),
                0,
                0,
                [],
                [],
            );
        } finally {
            $this->permissionRegistrar->setPermissionsTeamId($previousTeamId);
            $this->permissionRegistrar->forgetCachedPermissions();
        }
    }

    /**
     * lane/w-lot-a-1a's resolveCatalogueRoles() verbatim, minus the hard-coded
     * general_manager provisioning: NULL-or-tenant team predicate, tenant_id
     * PRESERVED as found, ordered name then id, lockForUpdate, and both refusals.
     *
     * @param  list<string>  $names
     * @return Collection<string, Role>
     */
    private function resolveCatalogueRoles(string $tenantId, array $names): Collection
    {
        $team = config('permission.column_names.team_foreign_key');
        /** @var Collection<int, Role> $roles */
        $roles = Role::query()
            ->where('guard_name', 'sanctum')
            ->whereIn('name', $names)
            ->where(fn (Builder $query): Builder => $query->whereNull($team)->orWhere($team, $tenantId))
            ->orderBy('name')
            ->orderBy('id')
            ->lockForUpdate()
            ->get();

        foreach ($roles->groupBy('name') as $name => $matches) {
            if ($name === 'general_manager' && $matches->contains(
                static fn (Role $role): bool => $role->getAttribute('provisioning_source') !== 'w-lot-a-1a',
            )) {
                throw new DomainException('unmarked_general_manager_collision');
            }
            if ($matches->count() !== 1) {
                throw new DomainException('ambiguous_legacy_role_collision');
            }
        }

        return $roles->keyBy('name');
    }

    /** @return list<string> */
    private function currentKeys(Role $role): array
    {
        /** @var list<string> $keys */
        $keys = $role->permissions()->toBase()->pluck('name')->all();

        return $keys;
    }

    /**
     * Grant the subset of $keys this role does not already hold, diffed against
     * the CALLER'S snapshot.
     *
     * $current is a parameter, not a re-read, and that is the whole point: the
     * eligibility decision and the grant set must be computed from ONE read of
     * role_has_permissions, taken after the lock. Two reads are two moments, and
     * the guarantee this command sells — "the role we decided about is the role
     * we wrote to" — is exactly the difference between them.
     *
     * @param  list<string>  $keys
     * @param  list<string>  $current  the post-lock snapshot the caller decided from
     * @return int the number of grants this call added (or would add, in a dry run)
     */
    private function grantMissing(Role $role, array $keys, array $current, bool $write): int
    {
        $missing = array_values(array_diff($keys, $current));
        if ($missing === []) {
            return 0;
        }
        if ($write) {
            // ONE batch per role — the snapshot was taken once, so the batch is
            // what the snapshot approved.
            $role->givePermissionTo($missing);
        }

        return count($missing);
    }
}
~~~

      …and the sentinel it throws, beside it:

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\Services;

use App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs\EnsurePermissionsResult;
use RuntimeException;

/**
 * Rolls the per-tenant transaction back while carrying the classification out.
 * A dry run is WRITE-free, not FAILURE-free: the outcome, the counters, the
 * marker and the exit code are identical to an applying run.
 */
final class DryRunRollback extends RuntimeException
{
    public function __construct(public readonly EnsurePermissionsResult $result)
    {
        parent::__construct('dry_run');
    }
}
~~~

      …and the fail-closed sentinel for the missing-admin case, beside them (plan gate r1 **B0b-5**):

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\Services;

use RuntimeException;

/**
 * The tenant has no resolvable `admin` role, so nothing in it holds the keys its
 * routes are now gated on. Spatie fails closed: that tenant is 403 for EVERY
 * caller on every route this wave gates, including the operator running the
 * deploy.
 *
 * It extends RuntimeException, NOT DomainException, deliberately. DomainException
 * maps to BLOCKED — "this tenant is in a state the command declines to change"
 * — and BLOCKED is a shape an operator may reasonably re-run past. A tenant with
 * no admin is not that: it is a FAILURE, it must exit non-zero on the applying
 * AND the dry-run path, and it must never let a deploy checklist write
 * `permissions_ensure=ok`.
 */
final class AdminRoleMissing extends RuntimeException
{
    public function __construct(public readonly string $tenantId)
    {
        parent::__construct('admin_role_missing');
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 3 — the two commands.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Console\Commands;

use App\Modules\Identity\Application\Services\EnsurePermissionsRunner;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\EnsurePermissionsOutcome;
use Illuminate\Console\Command;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Log;
use Spatie\Permission\PermissionRegistrar;

/**
 * The SINGLE-TENANT operator form, for repairing one tenant. The wave-0b
 * DEPLOY runs permissions:ensure-fleet (spec 4.3.5, rev-8 gate r7 M-1) —
 * `tenants:run permissions:ensure` is never valid, because tenants:run
 * discards child exit statuses and would report a partial deploy as clean.
 */
final class EnsurePermissions extends Command
{
    protected $signature = 'permissions:ensure
        {--keys=*     : permission keys to create if absent}
        {--grant-to=* : role names that must hold every named key (in addition to admin)}
        {--dry-run    : report the plan; write nothing}';

    protected $description = 'Additively create wave-0b permission keys and grant them where the role is still pristine.';

    public function __construct(
        private readonly EnsurePermissionsRunner $runner,
        private readonly PermissionRegistrar $permissionRegistrar,
    ) {
        parent::__construct();
    }

    public function handle(): int
    {
        $tenantId = $this->currentTenantId();
        if ($tenantId === null) {
            $this->error('permissions:ensure needs a tenant context. Run it under tenancy, or use permissions:ensure-fleet.');

            return self::FAILURE;
        }

        /** @var list<string> $keys */
        $keys = array_values(array_filter((array) $this->option('keys'), 'is_string'));
        /** @var list<string> $grantTo */
        $grantTo = array_values(array_filter((array) $this->option('grant-to'), 'is_string'));

        if ($keys === []) {
            $this->error('permissions:ensure requires at least one --keys= value.');

            return self::FAILURE;
        }

        $write = ! (bool) $this->option('dry-run');
        $result = $this->runner->run($tenantId, $keys, $grantTo, $write);

        $marker = $result->markerLine($tenantId, $write);
        $this->line($marker);
        Log::channel('stderr')->info($marker);

        if ($result->outcome === EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Failed && $result->reason === 'admin_role_missing') {
            // Loud, and the same on both paths. A dry run that cannot find admin
            // has found a real defect, not an absence of work.
            $this->error(sprintf(
                'tenant %s has no resolvable `admin` role. Every route this wave gates is 403 for EVERY caller in that '
                .'tenant, admin included, because Spatie fails closed on a key nobody holds. Do NOT record this deploy '
                .'as successful: resolve the missing role first, then re-run.',
                $tenantId,
            ));
        }

        foreach ($result->adminOnly as $role) {
            $this->warn(sprintf(
                'role `%s` has been customised in this tenant — the named keys were granted to `admin` only; '
                .'grant them deliberately in Settings → Roles, or they will arrive with the wave-1 *Re-apply template* action.',
                $role,
            ));
        }
        foreach ($result->missingRoles as $role) {
            $this->warn(sprintf('role `%s` does not exist in this tenant and was skipped; permissions:ensure creates no role.', $role));
        }

        return $result->outcome === EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Blocked || $result->outcome === EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Failed
            ? self::FAILURE
            : self::SUCCESS;
    }

    private function currentTenantId(): ?string
    {
        $id = tenant('id') ?? $this->permissionRegistrar->getPermissionsTeamId();

        return is_string($id) && $id !== '' ? $id : null;
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Console\Commands;

use App\Modules\Identity\Application\Services\EnsurePermissionsRunner;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\EnsurePermissionsOutcome;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\DTOs\FleetTenantOutcome;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Application\Services\TenantFleetRunner;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant;
use Illuminate\Console\Command;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Log;

/**
 * The FLEET form the wave-0b deploy runs.
 *
 * --keys and --grant-to are declared on THIS command and handed to the
 * per-tenant routine as PHP arrays, so tenants:run's --option=['k=v'] string
 * encoding never appears and cannot be mistyped into silence.
 *
 * Deleted in wave 1 with EnsurePermissions and EnsurePermissionsRunner.
 */
final class EnsurePermissionsFleet extends Command
{
    protected $signature = 'permissions:ensure-fleet
        {--tenants=*  : limit to these tenant ids or slugs; omit for every tenant}
        {--keys=*     : forwarded verbatim to the per-tenant ensure routine}
        {--grant-to=* : forwarded verbatim to the per-tenant ensure routine}
        {--dry-run    : report the plan for every tenant; write nothing}';

    protected $description = 'Run permissions:ensure across the tenant fleet, with per-tenant isolation and a non-zero exit on any failure.';

    public function __construct(
        private readonly TenantFleetRunner $fleet,
        private readonly EnsurePermissionsRunner $runner,
    ) {
        parent::__construct();
    }

    public function handle(): int
    {
        /** @var list<string> $onlyTenants */
        $onlyTenants = array_values(array_filter((array) $this->option('tenants'), 'is_string'));
        /** @var list<string> $keys */
        $keys = array_values(array_filter((array) $this->option('keys'), 'is_string'));
        /** @var list<string> $grantTo */
        $grantTo = array_values(array_filter((array) $this->option('grant-to'), 'is_string'));
        $write = ! (bool) $this->option('dry-run');

        if ($keys === []) {
            $this->error('permissions:ensure-fleet requires at least one --keys= value.');

            return self::FAILURE;
        }

        $result = $this->fleet->run($onlyTenants, 'PERMISSIONS-ENSURE', function (Tenant $tenant) use ($keys, $grantTo, $write): FleetTenantOutcome {
            $tenantId = (string) $tenant->getAttribute('id');
            $tenantResult = $this->runner->run($tenantId, $keys, $grantTo, $write);

            // `mode=` is rendered by the DTO, so the fleet and single-tenant
            // markers are byte-identical in shape.
            $marker = $tenantResult->markerLine($tenantId, $write);
            $this->line($marker);
            Log::channel('stderr')->info($marker);

            return match ($tenantResult->outcome) {
                EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Applied => FleetTenantOutcome::applied($tenantId, $tenantResult->reason),
                EnsurePermissionsOutcome::AlreadyCurrent => FleetTenantOutcome::alreadyCurrent($tenantId, $tenantResult->reason),
                EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Blocked => FleetTenantOutcome::blocked($tenantId, $tenantResult->reason),
                EnsurePermissionsOutcome::Failed => FleetTenantOutcome::failed($tenantId, $tenantResult->reason),
            };
        });

        $aggregate = $result->aggregateLine('PERMISSIONS-ENSURE');
        $this->line($aggregate);
        Log::channel('stderr')->info($aggregate);

        return $result->isGreen() ? self::SUCCESS : self::FAILURE;
    }
}
~~~

### Steps — part B: wire the lock into every runtime writer (0b-14)

> **The five obligations, per writer:** (i) open a transaction; (ii) `PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)` as its **first** statement; (iii) `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` the affected `roles` rows ordered by **`roles.name` ASC, then `roles.id` ASC**; (iv) **re-read** the decision inputs after the lock is held and decide from that read; (v) write and commit.
>
> **The one stated exception is `RoleController::store`.** It creates a row that does not yet exist, so there is nothing to `FOR UPDATE` and no prior grant set to re-read. Its obligation is exactly three steps in one transaction: **open the transaction; acquire the lock; INSERT the role, then apply its initial grant set.** A post-insert `FOR UPDATE` on a row the same transaction just created adds **no** serialization beyond the advisory lock it already holds — no other transaction can be between the two statements, because no other transaction holds the lock — so it is not required and **must not be added as ritual**. `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest` asserts this as a **named property**, not as an exemption from the lock.

- [ ] **Step 4 — `RoleController`, five methods. Written out as a COMPLETE recipe** (plan gate r2 **M2-2**). Rev 2 left a placeholder constructor list, `/* …existing checks… */` in `update()` and `destroy()`, an `assignRole()` closure referring to `$tenantId` and `$roleName` before either existed, and prose for `removeRole()`. What follows is derived line by line from `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php` at `52f5ad796`, so it compiles as written. Post-lane anchors, re-measured: `__construct` `:66-71`, `store` `:209` (signature) / `:213` (`Role::create`) / `:221` (`syncPermissions`), `update` `:239` / `:241` (`findOrFail`) / `:265` (`syncPermissions`), `destroy` `:283` / `:285` / `:312` (`$role->delete()`), `assignRole` `:364` / `:370-371` (team boundary) / `:373` (existing `DB::transaction`) / `:379` (existing role `FOR UPDATE`) / `:385`, `removeRole` `:422` / `:430`.

      **(0) Where `$tenantId` comes from — the question rev 2 never answered.** The controller has no `$tenantId` in scope in `store`, `update` or `destroy`. It has two existing sources, and they are not interchangeable:

      | Source | Used today at | Use it in |
      |---|---|---|
      | `$this->companyContext->requireCompany()->tenant_id` | `:143` | `store`, `update`, `destroy` — HTTP methods behind `SetPermissionsTeam`, where a company is always bound |
      | `$actor->tenant_id`, `$actor = $request->user()` | `:368-371` | `assignRole`, `removeRole` — they already resolve the actor and already set the registrar team from it |

      Do **not** introduce a third. `PermissionWriteLock::acquire()` takes the tenant id that keys the advisory lock; two different values are two serialization domains, which is the failure the whole task exists to prevent.

      **(1) The constructor.** The controller already has one at `:66-71` with five `private readonly` dependencies. Append a sixth — never `app()` (rule 13):
      ```php
      public function __construct(
          private readonly CompanyContext $companyContext,
          private readonly LotActionPermissionActivation $activation,
          private readonly CompanyConfigService $configService,
          private readonly GeneralManagerAssignmentGuard $generalManagerAssignmentGuard,
          private readonly PermissionRegistrar $permissionRegistrar,
          private readonly PermissionWriteLock $permissionWriteLock,
      ) {}
      ```
      and one import beside the existing ones at `:7-31`:
      ```php
      use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Locks\PermissionWriteLock;
      ```

      **(2) `store(StoreRoleRequest $request): JsonResponse`** — the create-path exception. The body at `:211-232` becomes:
      ```php
      $validated = $request->validated();
      $tenantId = (string) $this->companyContext->requireCompany()->tenant_id;

      $role = DB::transaction(function () use ($validated, $tenantId): Role {
          $this->permissionWriteLock->acquire($tenantId);

          $role = Role::create([
              'name' => $validated['name'],
              'guard_name' => 'sanctum',
          ]);

          assert($role instanceof Role);

          if (! empty($validated['permissions'])) {
              $role->syncPermissions($validated['permissions']);
          }

          return $role;
      });

      $role->load('permissions');

      return response()->json([
          'data' => (array) $this->roleData($role),
          'meta' => [
              'timestamp' => now()->toIso8601String(),
              'request_id' => $request->header('X-Request-ID', (string) uuid_create()),
          ],
      ], 201);
      ```
      `$role->load('permissions')` and the response move **outside** the closure deliberately: they read, they do not write, and holding the advisory lock across response construction lengthens the critical section for nothing. The `#[CrossTenantRoute]` attribute at `:208` is untouched.

      **(3) `update(UpdateRoleRequest $request, int $id): JsonResponse`.** The `findOrFail` at `:241` becomes the locked read, and **both existing refusals move inside the transaction** so they are evaluated against the row as re-read under the lock — that is obligation (iv), and it is the only behavioural change:
      ```php
      $tenantId = (string) $this->companyContext->requireCompany()->tenant_id;

      return DB::transaction(function () use ($request, $id, $tenantId): JsonResponse {
          $this->permissionWriteLock->acquire($tenantId);

          $role = Role::query()->where('id', $id)->lockForUpdate()->firstOrFail();

          if ($this->isMarkedProvisionedRole($role) && ($request->has('name') || $request->has('permissions'))) {
              return $this->provisionedRoleReadOnlyResponse();
          }

          // Prevent modifying system roles
          if ($this->isProtectedSystemRole($role) && $request->has('name') && $request->input('name') !== $role->name) {
              return response()->json([
                  'error' => [
                      'code' => 'SYSTEM_ROLE_PROTECTED',
                      'message' => 'System roles cannot be renamed.',
                  ],
              ], 422);
          }

          $validated = $request->validated();

          if (isset($validated['name'])) {
              $role->name = $validated['name'];
              $role->save();
          }

          if (isset($validated['permissions'])) {
              $role->syncPermissions($validated['permissions']);
          }

          $role->load('permissions');

          return response()->json([
              'data' => (array) $this->roleData($role),
              'meta' => [
                  'timestamp' => now()->toIso8601String(),
                  'request_id' => $request->header('X-Request-ID', (string) uuid_create()),
              ],
          ]);
      });
      ```
      **`Role::findOrFail($id)` becomes `Role::query()->where('id', $id)->lockForUpdate()->firstOrFail()` and takes NO order clause.** An equality on `roles.id` returns at most one row, so there is nothing to order, and the coverage test's row-order rule deliberately does not ask for one (see part C, Step 7). Spatie's team global scope still applies to `Role::query()`, so the `CrossTenantRoute` reasoning at `:238` is unchanged. **Returning a 422 from inside `DB::transaction` commits an empty transaction** — nothing was written before the refusal — which is correct and is why the refusals do not need to throw.

      **(4) `destroy(DeleteRoleRequest $request, int $id): JsonResponse`.** Same shape; both guards and the users-count check move under the lock, because the count is a decision input:
      ```php
      $tenantId = (string) $this->companyContext->requireCompany()->tenant_id;

      return DB::transaction(function () use ($request, $id, $tenantId): JsonResponse {
          $this->permissionWriteLock->acquire($tenantId);

          $role = Role::query()->where('id', $id)->lockForUpdate()->firstOrFail();

          if ($this->isMarkedProvisionedRole($role)) {
              return $this->provisionedRoleReadOnlyResponse();
          }

          // Prevent deleting system roles
          if ($this->isProtectedSystemRole($role)) {
              return response()->json([
                  'error' => [
                      'code' => 'SYSTEM_ROLE_PROTECTED',
                      'message' => 'System roles cannot be deleted.',
                  ],
              ], 422);
          }

          // Check if role has users — re-read UNDER the lock, because a concurrent
          // assignRole() would otherwise let a role be deleted a moment after it
          // stopped being empty.
          $usersCount = $this->countUsersForRole($role);
          if ($usersCount > 0) {
              return response()->json([
                  'error' => [
                      'code' => 'ROLE_HAS_USERS',
                      'message' => "Cannot delete role with {$usersCount} assigned user(s). Reassign users first.",
                  ],
              ], 422);
          }

          $role->delete();

          return response()->json([
              'data' => [
                  'message' => 'Role deleted successfully.',
              ],
              'meta' => [
                  'timestamp' => now()->toIso8601String(),
                  'request_id' => $request->header('X-Request-ID', (string) uuid_create()),
              ],
          ]);
      });
      ```

      **(5) `assignRole(AssignRoleRequest $request, string $userId): JsonResponse`** — the smallest diff of the five, because the transaction, the team boundary and the role `FOR UPDATE` already exist at `:370-379`. **One inserted line**, and nothing moves:
      ```php
      $validated = $request->validated();

      $actor = $request->user();
      abort_unless($actor instanceof User, 403);
      $previousTeam = $this->permissionRegistrar->getPermissionsTeamId();
      $this->permissionRegistrar->setPermissionsTeamId($actor->tenant_id);
      try {
          return DB::transaction(function () use ($request, $userId, $validated, $actor): JsonResponse {
              $this->permissionWriteLock->acquire((string) $actor->tenant_id);   // <-- 0b-14, the ONLY new line
              $user = User::query()->where('tenant_id', $actor->tenant_id)->where('id', $userId)->lockForUpdate()->firstOrFail();
              // …:375-412 unchanged, byte for byte: the membership read, the
              // activation abort, the Role FOR UPDATE at :379, both
              // assertAssignable calls, the RoleAssigned event and the response…
          });
      } finally {
          $this->permissionRegistrar->setPermissionsTeamId($previousTeam);
      }
      ```
      **Rev 2 claimed "the admin-population count moves inside it".** It does not: **there is no admin-population count in this method** at `52f5ad796` — the last-admin floor is a wave-2a deliverable (spec §4.6.3), and rev 2's sentence described code that does not exist. Nothing moves. The existing `Role::query()->where(team)->where('name', $validated['role'])->lockForUpdate()->first()` at `:379` stays exactly as it is, order clause and all (it has none, and needs none — single row by `roles.name`).

      **(6) `removeRole(AssignRoleRequest $request, string $userId): JsonResponse`** — the largest diff, because this method has **no transaction, no team boundary and no `FOR UPDATE` at all** today (`:422-455`). It gains all three, in `assignRole`'s exact shape:
      ```php
      public function removeRole(AssignRoleRequest $request, string $userId): JsonResponse
      {
          $validated = $request->validated();

          $actor = $request->user();
          abort_unless($actor instanceof User, 403);

          /** @var string $roleName */
          $roleName = $validated['role'];

          $previousTeam = $this->permissionRegistrar->getPermissionsTeamId();
          $this->permissionRegistrar->setPermissionsTeamId($actor->tenant_id);
          try {
              return DB::transaction(function () use ($request, $userId, $roleName, $actor): JsonResponse {
                  $this->permissionWriteLock->acquire((string) $actor->tenant_id);

                  $user = $this->resolveTenantUser($userId);

                  Role::query()
                      ->where(config('permission.column_names.team_foreign_key'), $actor->tenant_id)
                      ->where('name', $roleName)
                      ->where('guard_name', 'sanctum')
                      ->lockForUpdate()
                      ->first();

                  $user->removeRole($roleName);

                  // Privileged action — role revocation must be audit-logged with the
                  // actor and timestamp, mirroring assignRole().
                  event(new RoleRemoved(
                      targetUserId: $user->id,
                      roleName: $roleName,
                      companyId: $this->companyContext->requireCompany()->id,
                      actorUserId: $actor->id,
                      removedAt: now()->toIso8601String(),
                  ));

                  return response()->json([
                      'data' => [
                          'message' => "Role '{$roleName}' removed from user",
                          'user_id' => $user->id,
                          'roles' => $user->getRoleNames(),
                      ],
                      'meta' => [
                          'timestamp' => now()->toIso8601String(),
                          'request_id' => $request->header('X-Request-ID', (string) uuid_create()),
                      ],
                  ]);
              });
          } finally {
              $this->permissionRegistrar->setPermissionsTeamId($previousTeam);
          }
      }
      ```
      Two things this deliberately changes and one it deliberately does not. **It changes:** `$actor` is resolved and `abort_unless`-checked *before* the transaction rather than after the write (`:434-435` today), because the response now needs it inside the closure; and the `if ($actor instanceof User)` guard around the event collapses into that `abort_unless`, which is the same refusal one statement earlier — a `403` for a non-`User` principal either way, and PHPStan L8 needs the narrowing before the closure captures it. **It does not change:** the `RoleRemoved` payload, the response body, the status code, or `resolveTenantUser()`'s own behaviour.

      **Do not change any response body, status code, validation rule, `CrossTenantRoute` annotation or audit dispatch in this task.** The only changes are the transaction, the lock, the `FOR UPDATE`, and the two `$actor`-narrowing moves named above. The `#[CrossTenantRoute]` attribute on `store` (lane `:208`) stays exactly where it is.

- [ ] **Step 5 — `UserController`, two methods. The rev-1 line numbers were STALE and were corrected in rev 2** (plan gate r1 citation audit; re-measured at `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `52f5ad796` and **re-confirmed unchanged for rev 3**). Rev 1 said the transactions were at `:217`/`:357` and the writes at `:234`/`:384`; at the current lane tip they are:

      | Method | Signature | Existing `DB::transaction` | Write |
      |---|---:|---:|---:|
      | `store(CreateUserRequest $request)` | `:197` | **`:225`** | **`:268`** (`assignRole`) |
      | `update(UpdateUserRequest $request, string $id)` | `:318` | **`:375`** | **`:409`** (`syncRoles`) |

      Both already open a transaction **and already set the registrar team from `$currentUser->tenant_id` immediately before it** (`:222-223` and `:372-373`), so the change in each is **one inserted line**, first inside the existing closure — no read moves, because both methods already take every `FOR UPDATE` they need inside that closure (`store` at `:264-266`, `update` at `:383-388`):
      ```php
      // store — first statement inside the closure that opens at :225
      $this->permissionWriteLock->acquire((string) $currentUser->tenant_id);
      // update — first statement inside the closure that opens at :375
      $this->permissionWriteLock->acquire((string) $currentUser->tenant_id);
      ```
      **Both closures already capture `$currentUser`**, so neither `use` list changes: `store`'s at `:225` is `use ($validated, $currentUser, $companyId, $hasLocationGrant, $requestedLocations)` and `update`'s at `:375-382` is `use ($user, $validated, $currentUser, $request, $hasLocationGrant, $requestedLocations)`. Add the same two things `RoleController` needs: the `PermissionWriteLock` import, and one more `private readonly PermissionWriteLock $permissionWriteLock` on the existing constructor.

      Neither method needs a grant-set re-read — they assign a role to a **user**, they do not change a role's grants — so obligation (iv) is satisfied by the **role** `FOR UPDATE` each already takes. **Three other `DB::transaction` blocks in this file (`:501`, `:579`, `:661`) are not role writers and are not touched.**

- [ ] **Step 6 — `ResetTenantCommand`.** Spec §4.3.2's rev-8 M-2 ruling moves it from INITIALIZATION-ONLY to **LOCKED**. The command refuses only in production (`:29-35`), `--force` skips the confirmation (`:59-63`), and it drops and recreates the live schema with no maintenance mode (`:65-71`) — staging and development tenants routinely have active operators, so "no operator can be concurrent" was simply false for this row.

      **This command has NO constructor today** (plan gate r2 **M2-2**): it takes its one dependency by **method injection** on `handle(TenantInitializationService $initService)` at `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Commands/ResetTenantCommand.php:27`. Rev 2 wrote `$this->permissionWriteLock->acquire(...)` against a property that does not exist. Add the constructor — a `Command` subclass must call `parent::__construct()` or Symfony never registers its name:
      ```php
      public function __construct(
          private readonly PermissionWriteLock $permissionWriteLock,
      ) {
          parent::__construct();
      }
      ```
      and three imports beside the existing block at `:7-14`:
      ```php
      use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Locks\PermissionWriteLock;
      use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder;
      use Spatie\Permission\Models\Role;
      ```
      **Leave `handle(TenantInitializationService $initService)`'s method injection exactly as it is.** Mixing the two is normal in Laravel console commands, and converting `$initService` to a constructor dependency is a change this task has no reason to make.

      The correction itself is the small one: the `assignRole('admin')` loop at `:92-95` moves inside a transaction whose first statement is the lock, taken **after** the schema recreate and migrations:
      ```php
      $users = User::where('tenant_id', $tenant->id)->get();
      setPermissionsTeamId($tenant->id);
      DB::transaction(function () use ($tenant, $users): void {
          $this->permissionWriteLock->acquire((string) $tenant->id);
          $admin = Role::query()
              ->where('guard_name', 'sanctum')
              ->where('name', 'admin')
              ->where(fn (Builder $q): Builder => $q->whereNull('tenant_id')->orWhere('tenant_id', $tenant->id))
              ->lockForUpdate()
              ->firstOrFail();
          foreach ($users as $user) {
              $user->assignRole($admin);
              $this->line("  Assigned admin role to: {$user->email}");
          }
      });
      ```
