      **No order clause, deliberately:** `where('name', 'admin')` is an equality on `roles.name`, so this locks at most one row and the coverage test's row-order rule does not ask it to order anything (rev 2 wrote `orderBy('name')->orderBy('id')` here, which was harmless but suggested the multi-row rule applied where it does not). The explicit `setPermissionsTeamId($tenant->id)` the command already performs at `:91` **stays exactly where it is** — outside the transaction, before the lock, exactly as `RoleController::assignRole` does it.

      **Out of scope, deliberately and by name:** serialising the `DROP SCHEMA … CASCADE` itself. The lock cannot usefully span it — between the drop and the recreate the `roles` table does not exist, so no role-grant writer can be contending for anything the lock names, and holding one transaction across `DROP SCHEMA`, `CREATE SCHEMA` and a full migration run would block every other role writer in that tenant for the whole duration and would wrap DDL the migrator already transactions. This plan does **not** claim the reset is concurrency-safe as a whole; it claims only, and checkably, that the **role assignment** at the end can no longer interleave with another role-grant writer.

### Steps — part C: the tests

- [ ] **Step 7 — `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest`, at CALL-SITE / METHOD level** (plan gate r1 **BLOCKER B0b-3**).

      **What was wrong with rev 1, stated so the replacement is not read as a refactor.** Rev 1 enumerated **files** by raw substring, and asserted "this file mentions `PermissionWriteLock` somewhere". Three consequences, each of which defeats the test's whole purpose:
      1. a **new unlocked method** added to `RoleController` or `UserController` passes, because the file still mentions the lock elsewhere;
      2. the row-order assertion accepts **one** correctly ordered query anywhere in the file, not one per writer;
      3. `str_contains` matches inside comments and docblocks — so wave 1's `TemplateDeltaApplier`, whose docblock says it does **not** take the lock and relies on its callers, would be marked LOCKED by its own disclaimer (wave-1 plan gate r1 M1-3). A proof that a comment can satisfy is not a proof.

      **The replacement enumerates CALLS and attributes each to the METHOD that contains it,** using `nikic/php-parser`, which is already a direct dev dependency (`apps/api/composer.json` `require-dev` `nikic/php-parser: ^5.7`, vendored at `apps/api/vendor/nikic/php-parser`) and is therefore available to the test suite with no new package. An AST walk cannot match a comment or a string literal, so no narrowing heuristic is needed and none is written — which also removes rev 1's "resolve the false matches honestly" prose, the place a future agent would have been tempted to silence a real writer.

      **Measured now, at `dev` `630afa86f`, by exactly the walk below: twenty writer methods.** **Seven are runtime, thirteen are initialization** — `RoleController`'s four (`assignRole`, `removeRole`, `store`, `update`), `UserController`'s two (`store`, `update`) and `TenantInitializationService::assignDefaultRoles`; the other thirteen are the two tenant-migration `up()` writers, `ResetTenantCommand::handle` and the ten seeder methods. *(Plan gate r4 **minor 1**: rev 4's prose said nine and eleven. The fixture lists themselves were always correct and are unchanged — only this sentence's arithmetic was wrong, and it is the sentence a reviewer checks the fixture against.)* After the lane, the three additions are **one** locked writer (`LotActionPermissionDelta::execute`) plus **two** that inherit the lock from it (`provisionGeneralManager`, `synchronizeSeededRole`). After `lane/w-lot-a-1a` merges, `LotActionPermissionDelta`'s three writer methods join them and the seeder's two move to their lane line numbers; Phase 0's re-derivation is where that is captured. `RoleController::destroy` does **not** appear — `$role->delete()` writes neither `role_has_permissions` nor `model_has_roles` directly, it cascades — and it is nevertheless in `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` below, because the cascade deletes pivot rows and must be serialized against a concurrent grant. **A method that must hold the lock but is not itself a writer is declared, not discovered.**

      **The third category the ruling's two-way split needs.** `LotActionPermissionDelta` takes the lock in `execute()` and then writes from two **private helpers** — `provisionGeneralManager()` (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:…/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:154,157`) and `synchronizeSeededRole()` (`:175,177,180`) — while `execute()` itself acquires at `:54` and writes at `:83`. A flat "every writer method acquires the lock in its own body" rule would fail both helpers **falsely**. `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM` resolves that mechanically rather than by waiver: a helper may inherit its entry points' lock **only if** the test proves, from the same AST, that (a) **every declared entry point** acquires before it calls the helper, and (b) the declared list is the **complete** set of callers **anywhere in the scanned tree**. An inheriting helper that acquires a caller you did not declare fails.

      > **The map is helper ⇒ a LIST of entry points, and (b) is tree-wide, both from plan gate r2 M2-6.** Rev 2 had a scalar value and enumerated callers only within the helper's own file. That is a real proof for these two — both are `private`, so PHP itself forbids an out-of-file caller — but it is not a proof *in general*, and wave 1 is the case that breaks it: `TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo()` is a public method on its own class, and the wave-1 plan gives it **two** entry points (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:139` — *"two callers"* — and `:6513`, which pins them as `[PermissionSyncService::sync, RoleController::reapplyTemplate]`). Widening the shape now, while the fixture has two easy rows in it, is cheaper than widening it under pressure in the wave that needs it — see **Signature changes for wave 1**.

      First the walker, as its own support class so wave 1's `PermissionSyncService` and `TemplateDeltaApplier` inherit it rather than re-implement it:

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Architecture\Support;

use PhpParser\Node;
use PhpParser\NodeFinder;
use PhpParser\NodeTraverser;
use PhpParser\NodeVisitorAbstract;
use PhpParser\ParserFactory;
use RuntimeException;
use SplFileInfo;
use Symfony\Component\Finder\Finder;

/**
 * Enumerate, per METHOD, every call that writes `role_has_permissions` or
 * `model_has_roles`, plus every call that acquires the permission write lock —
 * from the AST, so a comment, a docblock or a string literal can never satisfy
 * either question (spec 4.3.2; plan gate r1 B0b-3).
 *
 * The unit of the census is `<relative path>::<method>`. A file is not a unit:
 * a file that mentions the lock once tells you nothing about the method added to
 * it last week. An anonymous class — every tenant migration is one — is keyed by
 * its path, which is unique and stable.
 */
final class PermissionWriterCensus
{
    /**
     * Instance-call names that write a grant or a role assignment. Spatie's
     * whole write surface for these two tables.
     */
    public const WRITER_METHODS = [
        'syncPermissions', 'givePermissionTo', 'revokePermissionTo',
        'assignRole', 'syncRoles', 'removeRole',
    ];

    /**
     * Static calls that create a catalogue row. Matched on the LAST name
     * segment, so both `Role::create(...)` and
     * `\Spatie\Permission\Models\Role::create(...)` are one match — and an
     * unrelated `Product::firstOrCreate(...)` is not a match at all, which is
     * what a raw `firstOrCreate` grep could not express.
     *
     * @var list<array{0: string, 1: string}>
     */
    public const WRITER_STATICS = [
        ['Role', 'create'], ['Role', 'firstOrCreate'],
        ['Permission', 'create'], ['Permission', 'firstOrCreate'],
    ];

    /**
     * Acquiring the tenant advisory lock. Three spellings, all real, none of
     * them a comment:
     *   - `$this->permissionWriteLock->acquire(...)`   the injected helper;
     *   - `PermissionWriteLock::acquire(...)`          a static call, if one
     *                                                  is ever added;
     *   - `$this->acquireTenantLock(...)`              lane/w-lot-a-1a's own
     *                                                  private helper, which
     *                                                  takes the SAME key and
     *                                                  the SAME hash function
     *                                                  (LotActionPermissionDelta
     *                                                  :131-135) — adopting the
     *                                                  key is what makes the two
     *                                                  writers one serialization
     *                                                  domain.
     */
    public const ACQUIRE_METHODS = ['acquire', 'acquireTenantLock'];

    /**
     * The FULLY QUALIFIED class names whose query root makes a
     * `lockForUpdate()` a **roles** lock (plan gate r3 **B3-1**).
     *
     * Rev 3 matched on the *last* name segment (`ROLE_QUERY_ROOTS = ['Role']`),
     * which is why the rev-4 rule resolves the alias through the file's `use`
     * map instead: a `Role` imported from some other namespace is not this
     * table, and a `Role` aliased (`use Spatie\Permission\Models\Role as
     * SpatieRole`) still is. Both spellings the tree actually contains are
     * listed; a third resolves to neither and is therefore **not** a roles
     * chain.
     *
     * Verified at `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `52f5ad796`: all three files holding a
     * `Role`-rooted `lockForUpdate()` import
     * `Spatie\Permission\Models\Role` —
     * `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:16`,
     * `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php:30`,
     * `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php:34`.
     *
     * @var list<string>
     */
    public const ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES = [
        'Spatie\Permission\Models\Role',
        'App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Role',
    ];

    /**
     * The table name that makes a query-builder root a roles chain.
     * `DB::table('roles')` — and its injected spelling `$this->db->table('roles')`
     * — is included because wave 1's `TemplateDeltaApplier` writes through the
     * query builder rather than the model. A `table('<anything else>')` literal
     * is **provably not** a roles chain and is ignored, which is what removes
     * three of gate r3's twenty-three false offenders on its own.
     */
    public const ROLE_TABLE = 'roles';

    /**
     * The lexical fail-closed trigger. An UNRESOLVABLE chain root is reported
     * as an `unresolved_role_candidate` — and therefore as a test offender —
     * only when the enclosing method mentions one of these anywhere in its own
     * AST-bounded source. Every other unresolvable chain is ignored, because
     * "I could not resolve this root" is not evidence that it is a roles query,
     * and treating it as one is what made rev 3's assertion mechanically red on
     * twenty-three methods that never touch the `roles` table.
     *
     * @var list<string>
     */
    public const ROLE_LEXICAL_MARKERS = ['roles', 'Role'];

    /**
     * @param  list<string>  $roots  absolute directories to scan
     * @return array<string, array{path: string, method: string, start: int, end: int, writes: list<array{name: string, line: int}>, acquires: list<int>, calls: list<array{name: string, line: int}>, roleLocks: list<array{line: int, verdict: 'roles'|'unresolved_role_candidate', multiRow: bool, ordered: bool}>}>
     */
    public function collect(array $roots): array
    {
        $parser = (new ParserFactory)->createForNewestSupportedVersion();
        $units = [];

        foreach ((new Finder)->files()->name('*.php')->in($roots) as $file) {
            /** @var SplFileInfo $file */
            $source = (string) $file->getContents();
            $ast = $parser->parse($source);
            if ($ast === null) {
                throw new RuntimeException('unparseable: '.$file->getPathname());
            }

            $relative = str_replace(base_path().'/', '', $file->getPathname());
            // The source string travels WITH the visitor (rev 4): the
            // fail-closed lexical check in recordRoleLock() reads the enclosing
            // method's own bytes, and re-opening the file from the test would
            // reintroduce exactly the "quote a later method" defect that
            // AST bounds removed.
            $visitor = new PermissionWriterVisitor($relative, $source);
            $traverser = new NodeTraverser;
            $traverser->addVisitor($visitor);
            $traverser->traverse($ast);

            foreach ($visitor->units as $key => $unit) {
                $units[$key] = $unit;
            }
        }

        ksort($units);

        return $units;
    }

    /**
     * @param  array<string, array{writes: list<array{name: string, line: int}>}>  $units
     * @return list<string> the `<path>::<method>` keys that contain at least one write
     */
    public function writerMethods(array $units): array
    {
        $keys = [];
        foreach ($units as $key => $unit) {
            if ($unit['writes'] !== []) {
                $keys[] = $key;
            }
        }
        sort($keys);

        return $keys;
    }

    /**
     * The exact source of ONE method, bounded by the AST rather than guessed.
     *
     * Rev 2 did this by `strpos(' function '.$method.'(')` and then took roughly
     * another 4 000 bytes, so a correctly ordered query in a LATER method could
     * satisfy a regex asked about THIS one (plan gate r2 B2-2). The parser
     * already knows where the method starts and ends — php-parser 5 records
     * `startFilePos` / `endFilePos` on every node by default, with no lexer
     * configuration — so the bounds are read off the node and the substring is
     * exact.
     *
     * @param  array{path: string, start: int, end: int}  $unit
     */
    public function methodSource(array $unit): string
    {
        $source = (string) file_get_contents(base_path($unit['path']));

        return substr($source, $unit['start'], $unit['end'] - $unit['start'] + 1);
    }

    /**
     * Every `<path>::<method>` ANYWHERE in the scanned tree that calls `$name`.
     *
     * This is the CROSS-FILE half of the LOCK_INHERITED_FROM proof (plan gate r2
     * M2-6). Rev 2 proved only that no other method IN THE SAME FILE called the
     * helper, which is sufficient for lane/w-lot-a-1a's two private helpers and
     * is NOT sufficient for a separate class — wave 1's TemplateDeltaApplier
     * lives in its own file and is called from another module entirely.
     *
     * Matching is by method NAME, so it OVER-approximates: an unrelated
     * `->applyTo()` on some other object is reported too. That direction is
     * safe — an over-approximation can only demand that more callers be
     * declared and proven, never fewer — and it is the only direction available
     * without type inference. A false hit is resolved by declaring that caller,
     * not by narrowing the matcher.
     *
     * @param  array<string, array{calls: list<array{name: string, line: int}>}>  $units
     * @return list<string>
     */
    public function callersOf(array $units, string $name): array
    {
        $keys = [];
        foreach ($units as $key => $unit) {
            foreach ($unit['calls'] as $call) {
                if ($call['name'] === $name) {
                    $keys[] = $key;
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        sort($keys);

        return $keys;
    }
}

/** @internal */
final class PermissionWriterVisitor extends NodeVisitorAbstract
{
    /** @var array<string, array{path: string, method: string, start: int, end: int, writes: list<array{name: string, line: int}>, acquires: list<int>, calls: list<array{name: string, line: int}>, roleLocks: list<array{line: int, verdict: 'roles'|'unresolved_role_candidate', multiRow: bool, ordered: bool}>}> */
    public array $units = [];

    /** @var list<string> */
    private array $methodStack = [];

    /** @var list<array{start: int, end: int}> */
    private array $boundsStack = [];

    /**
     * alias => fully qualified class name, from this file's `use` statements.
     *
     * @var array<string, string>
     */
    private array $useMap = [];

    /**
     * Variable name => the assignment expressions bound to it in the CURRENT
     * method, so a chain rooted in `$query` can be resolved to whatever
     * `$query = …` produced. Rebuilt on entering each `ClassMethod`.
     *
     * @var array<string, list<Node\Expr>>
     */
    private array $assignments = [];

    /**
     * Relation-method name => that method's source, for every method declared
     * on the class currently being walked. `$this->roles()` is decidable only
     * against the class that declares `roles()`; a method reached through a
     * trait is NOT in this map and is therefore UNRESOLVED, which is the
     * fail-closed direction.
     *
     * @var array<string, string>
     */
    private array $relationBodies = [];

    public function __construct(
        private readonly string $relativePath,
        private readonly string $source,
    ) {}

    public function enterNode(Node $node): ?Node
    {
        if ($node instanceof Node\Stmt\ClassLike) {
            $this->relationBodies = [];
            foreach ($node->getMethods() as $method) {
                $start = $method->getStartFilePos();
                $end = $method->getEndFilePos();
                $this->relationBodies[$method->name->toString()] = substr($this->source, $start, $end - $start + 1);
            }
        }

        // The `use` map is what turns the bare token `Role` into a decidable
        // question (plan gate r3 B3-1). Both plain and grouped imports.
        if ($node instanceof Node\Stmt\Use_) {
            foreach ($node->uses as $use) {
                $this->useMap[$use->getAlias()->toString()] = $use->name->toString();
            }

            return null;
        }
        if ($node instanceof Node\Stmt\GroupUse) {
            foreach ($node->uses as $use) {
                $this->useMap[$use->getAlias()->toString()] = $node->prefix->toString().'\\'.$use->name->toString();
            }

            return null;
        }

        if ($node instanceof Node\Stmt\ClassMethod) {
            $this->methodStack[] = $node->name->toString();
            // Same-method assignments, collected ONCE on entry so a chain
            // rooted in a variable can be resolved without a second traversal.
            $this->assignments = [];
            foreach ((new NodeFinder)->findInstanceOf($node, Node\Expr\Assign::class) as $assign) {
                /** @var Node\Expr\Assign $assign */
                if ($assign->var instanceof Node\Expr\Variable && is_string($assign->var->name)) {
                    $this->assignments[$assign->var->name][] = $assign->expr;
                }
            }
            // php-parser 5 records startFilePos/endFilePos on every node with no
            // lexer configuration, which is what makes the exact method body
            // available without re-scanning the source (plan gate r2 B2-2).
            $this->boundsStack[] = ['start' => $node->getStartFilePos(), 'end' => $node->getEndFilePos()];
            $this->unit();

            return null;
        }

        if ($node instanceof Node\Expr\MethodCall && $node->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
            $name = $node->name->toString();
            if (in_array($name, PermissionWriterCensus::WRITER_METHODS, true)) {
                $this->record('writes', $name, $node->getStartLine());
            }
            if (in_array($name, PermissionWriterCensus::ACQUIRE_METHODS, true)) {
                $this->record('acquires', $name, $node->getStartLine());
            }
            if ($name === 'lockForUpdate') {
                $this->recordRoleLock($node);
            }
            $this->record('calls', $name, $node->getStartLine());

            return null;
        }

        if ($node instanceof Node\Expr\StaticCall
            && $node->name instanceof Node\Identifier
            && $node->class instanceof Node\Name) {
            $class = $node->class->getLast();
            $method = $node->name->toString();
            foreach (PermissionWriterCensus::WRITER_STATICS as [$c, $m]) {
                if ($class === $c && $method === $m) {
                    $this->record('writes', $class.'::'.$method, $node->getStartLine());
                }
            }
            if ($class === 'PermissionWriteLock' && $method === 'acquire') {
                $this->record('acquires', $method, $node->getStartLine());
            }
        }

        return null;
    }

    public function leaveNode(Node $node): ?Node
    {
        if ($node instanceof Node\Stmt\ClassMethod) {
            array_pop($this->methodStack);
            array_pop($this->boundsStack);
        }

        return null;
    }

    /**
     * Classify ONE `lockForUpdate()` call by walking its own builder chain.
     *
     * **Rewritten for rev 4 — plan gate r3 BLOCKER B3-1.** Rev 3 asked three
     * questions; the first of them, *"is the chain rooted in the `roles` table
     * at all?"*, it answered `rootKnown = false` for every chain that is not a
     * static call, and then reported every such chain as an offender. Running
     * rev 3's own class mechanically over `app` + `database` at both `dev`
     * `630afa86f` and `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `52f5ad796` produced **twenty-three
     * offenders, none of which touches the `roles` table** — the disposition
     * table under the test enumerates all twenty-three. The assertion was
     * therefore red before a single wave-0b line was written.
     *
     * The rev-4 rule, and it is a THREE-valued one, which is the whole repair:
     *
     *   `ROLES`       — the root is PROVABLY the roles table. Four spellings:
     *                   (1) `Role::query()` / `Role::where(…)` where the alias
     *                       `Role` resolves through this file's `use` map to a
     *                       class in ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES;
     *                   (2) `DB::table('roles')` — a static call whose first
     *                       argument is the literal ROLE_TABLE;
     *                   (3) `$anything->table('roles')` — the injected
     *                       query-builder spelling, same literal;
     *                   (4) `$this->roles()` — a relation call on `$this` whose
     *                       same-class relation method mentions a
     *                       ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES class;
     *                   plus (5) a VARIABLE assigned, in this same method body,
     *                   from any of (1)–(4).
     *
     *   `NOT_ROLES`   — the root is PROVABLY something else: a static call on a
     *                   class that resolves to a non-role class, a
     *                   `table('<some other literal>')`, or a variable assigned
     *                   from one of those in this method. **Ignored entirely.**
     *                   This alone disposes of twenty of the twenty-three:
     *                   `$stockLevelQuery = StockLevel::query()`,
     *                   `$query = Voucher::query()`,
     *                   `$this->db->table('pos_terminals')`, and so on.
     *
     *   `UNRESOLVED`  — everything else: `$template->accounts()`,
     *                   `$this->rowQuery(...)`, a property fetch, a function
     *                   call. **Fail-closed, but only where failing closed can
     *                   mean anything**: it is recorded as an
     *                   `unresolved_role_candidate` — and refused by the test —
     *                   IF the enclosing method mentions `roles` or `Role`
     *                   anywhere in its own AST-bounded source
     *                   (ROLE_LEXICAL_MARKERS); otherwise it is ignored. A
     *                   method that never writes the word `roles` cannot be
     *                   locking the `roles` table through a variable it never
     *                   names, and pretending otherwise is what produced the
     *                   twenty-three.
     *
     * Two further corrections gate r3 asked for, both in the classifier below:
     *
     *   - **`whereIn` is MONOTONIC.** Rev 3 mutated one `$multiRow` flag in
     *     chain order, so `whereIn('name', $x)->where('id', '=', $y)` demoted
     *     itself to single-row and escaped the order rule. Rev 4 accumulates
     *     `$sawWhereIn` and `$sawEquality` independently and derives
     *     `multiRow = $sawWhereIn || ! $sawEquality` — order-independent, and
     *     an equality can never cancel a set predicate.
     *   - **Order direction is ASSERTED, not assumed.** The lane's order is
     *     `roles.name ASC` then `roles.id ASC`. `orderBy('name')` (one
     *     argument, Laravel's default) and `orderBy('name', 'asc')` count;
     *     `orderBy('name', 'desc')` and `orderByDesc('name')` do NOT. Rev 3
     *     accepted any second argument, so a descending order satisfied a rule
     *     whose entire purpose is that every participant walks the same rows in
     *     the same direction.
     */
    private function recordRoleLock(Node\Expr\MethodCall $lock): void
    {
        // Walk the chain inwards, then reverse it into source order.
        $chain = [];
        $cursor = $lock->var;
        while ($cursor instanceof Node\Expr\MethodCall) {
            if ($cursor->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
                $chain[] = $cursor;
            }
            $cursor = $cursor->var;
        }
        $chain = array_reverse($chain);

        // The root node may itself carry the decisive information
        // (Role::query(), DB::table('roles')), so it joins the chain for the
        // predicate walk below.
        if ($cursor instanceof Node\Expr\StaticCall) {
            array_unshift($chain, $cursor);
        }

        $verdict = $this->classifyRoot($cursor, $chain, 0);

        if ($verdict === 'NOT_ROLES') {
            return;
        }

        if ($verdict === 'UNRESOLVED') {
            if (! $this->methodMentionsRoles()) {
                // Provably-irrelevant: ignored, and this is the single line
                // that turns gate r3's twenty-three false offenders into zero.
                return;
            }
            $this->record2('roleLocks', [
                'line' => $lock->getStartLine(),
                'verdict' => 'unresolved_role_candidate',
                'multiRow' => true,
                'ordered' => false,
            ]);

            return;
        }

        // verdict === 'ROLES'
        $sawWhereIn = false;
        $sawEquality = false;
        $orderNameAt = null;
        $orderIdAt = null;

        foreach ($chain as $i => $call) {
            if (! $call->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
                continue;
            }
            $method = $call->name->toString();
            $first = isset($call->args[0]) && $call->args[0] instanceof Node\Arg
                && $call->args[0]->value instanceof Node\Scalar\String_
                ? $call->args[0]->value->value
                : null;

            if ($method === 'where' && in_array($first, ['name', 'id'], true)) {
                // where('name', $x) is an equality. where('name', '=', $x) is
                // too; where('name', '!=', $x) or 'like' is not.
                $twoArg = count($call->args) === 2;
                $threeArgEquals = count($call->args) === 3
                    && $call->args[1] instanceof Node\Arg
                    && $call->args[1]->value instanceof Node\Scalar\String_
                    && $call->args[1]->value->value === '=';
                if ($twoArg || $threeArgEquals) {
                    $sawEquality = true;
                }
            }
            if ($method === 'whereIn' && in_array($first, ['name', 'id'], true)) {
                $sawWhereIn = true;
            }
            if ($method === 'orderBy' && $this->isAscending($call)) {
                if ($first === 'name' && $orderNameAt === null) {
                    $orderNameAt = $i;
                }
                if ($first === 'id' && $orderIdAt === null) {
                    $orderIdAt = $i;
                }
            }
        }

        // MONOTONIC: a set predicate anywhere in the chain wins outright, and a
        // chain with no predicate on name/id at all is multi-row by default.
        $multiRow = $sawWhereIn || ! $sawEquality;
        $ordered = $orderNameAt !== null && $orderIdAt !== null && $orderNameAt < $orderIdAt;

        $this->record2('roleLocks', [
            'line' => $lock->getStartLine(),
            'verdict' => 'roles',
            'multiRow' => $multiRow,
            'ordered' => $ordered,
        ]);
    }

    /**
     * `orderBy('x')` and `orderBy('x', 'asc')` are ascending; anything else is
     * not. Case-insensitive, because `'ASC'` is legal Laravel.
     */
    private function isAscending(Node\Expr\MethodCall $call): bool
    {
        if (count($call->args) === 1) {
            return true;
        }
        if (count($call->args) !== 2 || ! $call->args[1] instanceof Node\Arg) {
            return false;
        }
        $direction = $call->args[1]->value;

        return $direction instanceof Node\Scalar\String_
            && strtolower($direction->value) === 'asc';
    }

    /**
     * ROLES / NOT_ROLES / UNRESOLVED for one chain root.
     *
     * `$depth` bounds the variable-assignment recursion: a variable assigned
     * from a variable assigned from a variable is resolved two hops and then
     * gives up as UNRESOLVED, which is the fail-closed direction.
     *
     * @param  list<Node\Expr\MethodCall|Node\Expr\StaticCall>  $chain
     * @return 'ROLES'|'NOT_ROLES'|'UNRESOLVED'
     */
    private function classifyRoot(?Node $root, array $chain, int $depth): string
    {
        // (3) $anything->table('roles') — the injected query-builder spelling.
        // Checked against the WHOLE chain, because the table() call is a link
        // in it rather than the root node.
        foreach ($chain as $call) {
            if ($call instanceof Node\Expr\MethodCall
                && $call->name instanceof Node\Identifier
                && $call->name->toString() === 'table') {
                $literal = $this->firstStringArg($call);
                if ($literal === PermissionWriterCensus::ROLE_TABLE) {
                    return 'ROLES';
                }
                if ($literal !== null) {
                    return 'NOT_ROLES';
                }
            }
        }

        // (1) and (2) — a static call root.
        if ($root instanceof Node\Expr\StaticCall && $root->class instanceof Node\Name) {
            if ($root->name instanceof Node\Identifier && $root->name->toString() === 'table') {
                $literal = $this->firstStringArg($root);
                if ($literal === PermissionWriterCensus::ROLE_TABLE) {
                    return 'ROLES';
                }
                if ($literal !== null) {
                    return 'NOT_ROLES';
                }

                return 'UNRESOLVED';
            }

            return $this->resolvesToRoleClass($root->class) ? 'ROLES' : 'NOT_ROLES';
        }

        // (4) $this->roles() — a relation on the enclosing class.
        if ($root instanceof Node\Expr\MethodCall
            && $root->var instanceof Node\Expr\Variable
            && $root->var->name === 'this'
            && $root->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
            return $this->relationReturnsRole($root->name->toString());
        }

        // (5) a variable assigned in this same method body.
        if ($root instanceof Node\Expr\Variable && is_string($root->name) && $depth < 2) {
            $verdicts = [];
            foreach ($this->assignments[$root->name] ?? [] as $expr) {
                $subChain = [];
                $cursor = $expr;
                while ($cursor instanceof Node\Expr\MethodCall) {
                    if ($cursor->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
                        $subChain[] = $cursor;
                    }
                    $cursor = $cursor->var;
                }
                $subChain = array_reverse($subChain);
                if ($cursor instanceof Node\Expr\StaticCall) {
                    array_unshift($subChain, $cursor);
                }
                $verdicts[] = $this->classifyRoot($cursor, $subChain, $depth + 1);
            }

            if ($verdicts === []) {
                return 'UNRESOLVED';
            }
            // Fail-closed across multiple assignments: one ROLES arm makes the
            // whole variable a roles chain; an UNRESOLVED arm keeps it
            // unresolved; only an all-NOT_ROLES set is dismissed.
            if (in_array('ROLES', $verdicts, true)) {
                return 'ROLES';
            }
            if (in_array('UNRESOLVED', $verdicts, true)) {
                return 'UNRESOLVED';
            }

            return 'NOT_ROLES';
        }

        return 'UNRESOLVED';
    }

    /** The first argument of a call, if it is a string literal. */
    private function firstStringArg(Node\Expr\StaticCall|Node\Expr\MethodCall $call): ?string
    {
        if (! isset($call->args[0]) || ! $call->args[0] instanceof Node\Arg) {
            return null;
        }
        $value = $call->args[0]->value;

        return $value instanceof Node\Scalar\String_ ? $value->value : null;
    }

    /**
     * Does this class NAME resolve, through the file's `use` map, to one of
     * ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES? A fully qualified name is taken as written; a bare
     * alias is looked up; an unknown alias is NOT a role class, because an
     * unresolved alias means the file never imported it.
     */
    private function resolvesToRoleClass(Node\Name $name): bool
    {
        $written = $name->toString();
        if ($name->isFullyQualified()) {
            return in_array(ltrim($written, '\\'), PermissionWriterCensus::ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES, true);
        }
        $resolved = $this->useMap[$written] ?? null;

        return $resolved !== null
            && in_array($resolved, PermissionWriterCensus::ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES, true);
    }

    /**
     * `$this-><relation>()`: ROLES when the same file declares that relation
     * method and its body names a ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES class; NOT_ROLES when the
     * file declares it and does not; UNRESOLVED when the file does not declare
     * it at all — the HasRoles-trait case, where the method is inherited and
     * the AST of this file cannot see it. UNRESOLVED then falls through to the
     * lexical fail-closed arm, which a method calling `$this->roles()`
     * necessarily trips.
     *
     * @return 'ROLES'|'NOT_ROLES'|'UNRESOLVED'
     */
    private function relationReturnsRole(string $relation): string
    {
        $declared = $this->relationBodies[$relation] ?? null;
        if ($declared === null) {
            return 'UNRESOLVED';
        }
        foreach (PermissionWriterCensus::ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES as $class) {
            $short = substr((string) strrchr($class, '\\'), 1);
            if (str_contains($declared, $short.'::class') && ($this->useMap[$short] ?? null) === $class) {
                return 'ROLES';
            }
        }

        return 'NOT_ROLES';
    }

    /**
     * Does the CURRENT method's own source mention `roles` or `Role`? This is
     * the fail-closed trigger and nothing else — it never promotes a chain to
     * ROLES, it only decides whether an UNRESOLVED one is worth refusing.
     */
    private function methodMentionsRoles(): bool
    {
        $bounds = end($this->boundsStack);
        if ($bounds === false) {
            return false;
        }
        $body = substr($this->source, $bounds['start'], $bounds['end'] - $bounds['start'] + 1);

        foreach (PermissionWriterCensus::ROLE_LEXICAL_MARKERS as $marker) {
            if (preg_match('/\b'.preg_quote($marker, '/').'\b/', $body) === 1) {
                return true;
            }
        }

        return false;
    }

    private function key(): ?string
    {
        $method = end($this->methodStack);

        return $method === false ? null : $this->relativePath.'::'.$method;
    }

    private function unit(): void
    {
        $key = $this->key();
        if ($key !== null && ! isset($this->units[$key])) {
            $bounds = end($this->boundsStack);
            $this->units[$key] = [
                'path' => $this->relativePath,
                'method' => (string) end($this->methodStack),
                'start' => $bounds === false ? 0 : $bounds['start'],
                'end' => $bounds === false ? 0 : $bounds['end'],
                'writes' => [],
                'acquires' => [],
                'calls' => [],
                'roleLocks' => [],
            ];
        }
    }

    private function record(string $bucket, string $name, int $line): void
    {
        $key = $this->key();
        if ($key === null) {
            return;
        }
        $this->unit();
        $this->units[$key][$bucket][] = $bucket === 'acquires' ? $line : ['name' => $name, 'line' => $line];
    }

    /** @param array<string, mixed> $entry */
    private function record2(string $bucket, array $entry): void
    {
        $key = $this->key();
        if ($key === null) {
            return;
        }
        $this->unit();
        $this->units[$key][$bucket][] = $entry;
    }
}
~~~

      Then the test:

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Identity;

use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Test;
use Tests\Architecture\Support\PermissionWriterCensus;
use Tests\TestCase;

/**
 * LOCKED ∪ LOCK_INHERITED_FROM ∪ INITIALIZATION_ONLY = the AST-enumerated set of
 * role-grant writer METHODS, and the three classes are pairwise DISJOINT
 * (spec 4.3.2, as corrected by plan gate r1 B0b-3).
 *
 * A writer METHOD added later belongs to none of the three and fails the build
 * with its path, its method name and the line of its first write — whether it is
 * the twenty-first or the fiftieth, and whether or not the file it lives in
 * already mentions the lock somewhere else.
 */
final class PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest extends TestCase
{
    /**
     * Runtime writer methods: reachable from an authenticated HTTP request, or
     * from a console command an operator runs against a live tenant that already
     * has operators in it. Each must call an acquire BEFORE its first write.
     *
     * RoleController::destroy carries no Spatie write call — `$role->delete()`
     * cascades — and is nevertheless declared, because the cascade deletes
     * role_has_permissions rows and must be serialized against a concurrent
     * grant. That is why this list is DECLARED rather than derived: it is a
     * superset of the enumerated writers by design, and
     * every_declared_entry_point_acquires_before_its_first_write covers both.
     *
     * @var list<string>
     */
    private const LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS = [
        'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::store',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::update',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::destroy',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::assignRole',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::removeRole',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php::store',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php::update',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/EnsurePermissionsRunner.php::run',
        'app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Commands/ResetTenantCommand.php::handle',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php::execute',
    ];

    /**
     * Helpers that WRITE but do not acquire, because EVERY entry point that
     * reaches them already holds the lock. NOT a waiver: the test proves, from
     * the same AST, that each declared entry point acquires before it calls the
     * helper AND that the declared list is the COMPLETE set of callers anywhere
     * in the scanned tree.
     *
     * The value is a LIST, not a single key (plan gate r2 M2-6). Both of
     * lane/w-lot-a-1a's helpers happen to have exactly one caller, so rev 2's
     * scalar shape fit them — but it could not express wave 1's
     * TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo, whose own plan gives it TWO entry points
     * (PermissionSyncService::sync and RoleController::reapplyTemplate,
     * docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:139,6513). A shape that
     * cannot represent the next case is a shape that will be widened under
     * pressure by the agent who meets it.
     *
     * @var array<string, list<string>>  helper => EVERY entry point whose lock it inherits
     */
    private const LOCK_INHERITED_FROM = [
        'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php::provisionGeneralManager' => [
            'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php::execute',
        ],
        'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php::synchronizeSeededRole' => [
            'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php::execute',
        ],
    ];

    /**
     * Provisioning, demo-seeder, legacy-seeder and once-per-database migration
     * writer METHODS. CLOSED and SHRINK-ONLY: a new entry needs an owner ruling,
     * and the set is pinned by EXACT equality so the exemption can only shrink.
     *
     * ResetTenantCommand::handle is NOT here — spec 4.3.2's rev-8 M-2 ruling
     * moved it to LOCKED, and this fixture shrank by exactly one row.
     *
     * PermissionSeeder::run IS here, and leaves in Task 10 of this wave, which
     * deletes the file. That is the fixture's second shrink and the only change
     * Task 10 makes to this test.
     *
     * @var list<string>
     */
    private const INITIALIZATION_ONLY = [
        'app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenantInitializationService.php::assignDefaultRoles',
        'database/seeders/DatabaseSeeder.php::createUsers',
        'database/seeders/DemoTenantSeeder.php::assignAdminRoleAndMembership',
        'database/seeders/DemoTenantSeeder.php::ensureTechnicianUser',
        'database/seeders/CoffeeShopSeeder.php::createTestUsers',
        'database/seeders/ParapharmacySeeder.php::createTestUsers',
        'database/seeders/DemoPharmacySeeder.php::seedTunisiaCashiers',
        'database/seeders/DemoPharmacySeeder.php::seedRoleCoverageUsers',
        'database/seeders/PermissionSeeder.php::run',
        'database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php::createPermissionsFrom',
        'database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php::createLegacyRoles',
        'database/migrations/tenant/2025_12_13_000001_ensure_settings_permissions.php::up',
        'database/migrations/tenant/2026_07_16_100100_register_manage_location_access_permission.php::up',
    ];

    /** @var array<string, array{path: string, method: string, start: int, end: int, writes: list<array{name: string, line: int}>, acquires: list<int>, calls: list<array{name: string, line: int}>, roleLocks: list<array{line: int, verdict: 'roles'|'unresolved_role_candidate', multiRow: bool, ordered: bool}>}> */
    private array $units;

    private PermissionWriterCensus $census;

    protected function setUp(): void
    {
        parent::setUp();
        $this->census = new PermissionWriterCensus;
        $this->units = $this->census->collect([base_path('app'), base_path('database')]);
    }

    #[Test]
    public function the_three_classes_partition_the_enumerated_writer_methods(): void
    {
        $enumerated = (new PermissionWriterCensus)->writerMethods($this->units);
        $declared = [
            ...self::LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS,
            ...array_keys(self::LOCK_INHERITED_FROM),
            ...self::INITIALIZATION_ONLY,
        ];
        sort($declared);

        $unclassified = array_values(array_diff($enumerated, $declared));
        $this->assertSame(
            [],
            $unclassified,
            "these methods write a role grant and are in none of LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS, LOCK_INHERITED_FROM or "
            ."INITIALIZATION_ONLY:\n  ".implode("\n  ", array_map(
                fn (string $key): string => $key.'  ->  '.implode(', ', array_map(
                    static fn (array $w): string => $w['name'].'@'.$w['line'],
                    $this->units[$key]['writes'],
                )),
                $unclassified,
            )),
        );

        // The other direction: a declared entry that is no longer a writer is a
        // stale exemption, and a stale exemption is how a fixture stops shrinking.
        // LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS is deliberately a superset (see its docblock), so
        // only the two exemption lists are pinned in this direction.
        $exempt = [...array_keys(self::LOCK_INHERITED_FROM), ...self::INITIALIZATION_ONLY];
        sort($exempt);
        $this->assertSame(
            [],
            array_values(array_diff($exempt, $enumerated)),
            'an exempted method that no longer writes must be REMOVED from its fixture, not carried.',
        );
    }

    #[Test]
    public function the_three_classes_are_pairwise_disjoint(): void
    {
        $locked = self::LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS;
        $inherited = array_keys(self::LOCK_INHERITED_FROM);

        $this->assertSame([], array_values(array_intersect($locked, $inherited)));
        $this->assertSame([], array_values(array_intersect($locked, self::INITIALIZATION_ONLY)));
        $this->assertSame([], array_values(array_intersect($inherited, self::INITIALIZATION_ONLY)));
    }

    /**
     * THE assertion. Not "the file mentions the lock" — "THIS METHOD acquires,
     * and acquires BEFORE its first write".
     */
    #[Test]
    public function every_declared_entry_point_acquires_before_its_first_write(): void
    {
        foreach (self::LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS as $key) {
            $this->assertArrayHasKey($key, $this->units, "{$key} does not exist; the fixture is stale.");
            $unit = $this->units[$key];

            $this->assertNotSame(
                [],
                $unit['acquires'],
                "{$key} is a locked writer and never calls acquire(). A file-level mention elsewhere is not a lock: "
                .'an equality check that is not serialized against the operator edit path is not a guard.',
            );

            if ($unit['writes'] === []) {
                continue; // declared for its cascade (RoleController::destroy), nothing to order against
            }

            $firstAcquire = min($unit['acquires']);
            $firstWrite = min(array_column($unit['writes'], 'line'));
            $this->assertLessThan(
                $firstWrite,
                $firstAcquire,
                "{$key} writes at line {$firstWrite} but acquires at line {$firstAcquire}. Obligation (ii): the lock is "
                .'the FIRST statement in the transaction, and a decision read after a write it did not serialize is not a decision.',
            );
        }
    }

    /**
     * An inheriting helper's DECLARED entry points are ALL of its callers, and
     * every one of them acquires before calling it.
     *
     * Rev 2 asserted (b) only within the helper's own FILE. That is sufficient
     * for a private helper — PHP guarantees no caller outside the class — but it
     * is not a general proof, and wave 1's TemplateDeltaApplier is a separate
     * public class in its own file. The caller enumeration is now tree-wide
     * (PermissionWriterCensus::callersOf), and the file-local test survives as
     * the stricter of the two only because the current helpers are private.
     * (Plan gate r2 M2-6.)
     */
    #[Test]
    public function an_inheriting_helper_declares_every_caller_and_each_one_locks_first(): void
    {
        foreach (self::LOCK_INHERITED_FROM as $helperKey => $entryKeys) {
            $this->assertArrayHasKey($helperKey, $this->units, "{$helperKey} does not exist; the fixture is stale.");
            $this->assertNotSame([], $entryKeys, "{$helperKey} declares no entry point, so it inherits nothing.");

            [, $helper] = explode('::', $helperKey, 2);

            // (a) EVERY declared entry point acquires BEFORE it calls the helper.
            foreach ($entryKeys as $entryKey) {
                $this->assertArrayHasKey($entryKey, $this->units, "{$entryKey} does not exist; the fixture is stale.");
                $entry = $this->units[$entryKey];
                $callLines = array_column(array_filter(
                    $entry['calls'],
                    static fn (array $c): bool => $c['name'] === $helper,
                ), 'line');
                $this->assertNotSame([], $callLines, "{$entryKey} does not call {$helper}(); the inheritance claim is false.");
                $this->assertNotSame([], $entry['acquires'], "{$entryKey} does not acquire.");
                $this->assertLessThan(min($callLines), min($entry['acquires']), "{$entryKey} calls {$helper}() before acquiring.");
            }

            // (b) The declared list is COMPLETE, across the whole scanned tree —
            // not merely across the helper's own file. An inheriting helper with
            // one undeclared caller inherits nothing, and the undeclared caller
            // is exactly the one nobody thought about.
            $actual = $this->census->callersOf($this->units, $helper);
            $undeclared = array_values(array_diff($actual, $entryKeys, [$helperKey]));
            $this->assertSame(
                [],
                $undeclared,
                "these methods also call {$helper}() and are not declared as entry points of {$helperKey}:\n  "
                .implode("\n  ", $undeclared)
                ."\nEither add each to LOCK_INHERITED_FROM['{$helperKey}'] (and prove it acquires first), or make the "
                .'helper acquire for itself. Matching is by method NAME and therefore over-approximates: an unrelated '
                .'call of the same name is resolved by DECLARING it, never by narrowing the matcher.',
            );
        }
    }

    /**
     * The named create-path property. RoleController::store is NOT exempt from
     * the lock; it is exempt from obligations (iii) and (iv), because the row it
     * locks does not exist yet.
     */
    #[Test]
    public function the_create_path_takes_the_lock_and_does_not_pretend_to_lock_a_row_it_is_about_to_insert(): void
    {
        $key = 'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::store';
        $unit = $this->units[$key];

        $this->assertNotSame([], $unit['acquires'], 'store() must take the lock.');
        $this->assertContains('Role::create', array_column($unit['writes'], 'name'));
        $this->assertSame(
            [],
            array_values(array_filter($unit['calls'], static fn (array $c): bool => $c['name'] === 'lockForUpdate')),
            'a post-insert FOR UPDATE on the row this transaction just created buys nothing beyond the advisory lock it '
            .'already holds, and must not be added as ritual.',
        );
    }

    /**
     * The lane row order, on the queries the order is ABOUT.
     *
     * Two narrowings on rev 2, both from plan gate r2 B2-2, and neither of them
     * a weakening:
     *
     *  1. **Per QUERY, not per method.** Rev 2 counted `orderBy` calls across the
     *     whole method and then regex-matched a method body, so a method holding
     *     one ordered and one unordered roles lock passed on the ordered one.
     *     The census now classifies each `lockForUpdate()` chain on its own.
     *
     *  2. **Multi-row roles queries ONLY.** Rev 2 applied the assertion to every
     *     locked or inheriting method, and was therefore RED on the lane it makes
     *     an entry condition: LotActionPermissionDelta::provisionGeneralManager
     *     locks a single row by `where('name', GeneralManager)` and orders
     *     nothing (lane/w-lot-a-1a:.../LotActionPermissionDelta.php:142-143), as
     *     do RoleController::assignRole (:379) and UserController::store (:266).
     *     A one-row result set has no order to agree about, and demanding one
     *     would have meant editing W-LOT-owned production code this wave does not
     *     list as a modification. The lane already draws exactly this line: its
     *     ONE multi-row roles lock, UserController::update's
     *     `whereIn('name', $roles)` at :388, DOES carry
     *     `orderBy('name')->orderBy('id')`.
     *
     * The rule, stated mechanically so it is not a judgement call: a
     * `lockForUpdate()` chain is subject to the lane order when its root is a
     * PROVABLY `roles` query AND it carries no equality predicate on
     * `roles.name` or `roles.id` that limits it to one row — where an equality
     * predicate is `where('name'|'id', $x)` or `where('name'|'id', '=', $x)`,
     * and any `whereIn('name'|'id', ...)` makes it multi-row regardless and
     * IRREVERSIBLY. Ascending order is asserted, not assumed.
     *
     * A chain whose root is provably NOT `roles` is ignored. A chain whose root
     * cannot be resolved at all is ignored UNLESS its method also mentions
     * `roles`/`Role`, in which case it is refused by name as an
     * `unresolved_role_candidate` (plan gate r3 **B3-1**: rev 3 refused EVERY
     * unresolvable chain, which is twenty-three methods that never touch the
     * table — see the disposition table under this test).
     */
    #[Test]
    public function every_multi_row_roles_lock_uses_the_lane_row_order(): void
    {
        $offenders = [];

        // Every unit in the tree, not only the declared writers: the tree's one
        // multi-row roles lock at the lane tip lives in
        // LotActionPermissionDelta::resolveCatalogueRoles, which writes nothing
        // and is therefore in none of the three fixtures. Scoping this assertion
        // to declared writers would have skipped the very query it is about.
        foreach ($this->units as $key => $unit) {
            foreach ($unit['roleLocks'] as $lock) {
                if ($lock['verdict'] === 'unresolved_role_candidate') {
                    $offenders[] = "{$key}@{$lock['line']}: the chain root of this lockForUpdate() could not be "
                        .'resolved from the AST, and this method names roles/Role, so it cannot be dismissed. '
                        .'Rewrite the root so it is decidable — Role::query(), DB::table(\'roles\'), or a variable '
                        ."assigned from one of those in this same method.\n"
                        .$this->census->methodSource($unit);

                    continue;
                }
                if (! $lock['multiRow'] || $lock['ordered']) {
                    continue;
                }
                $offenders[] = "{$key}@{$lock['line']}: a roles FOR UPDATE that can return more than one row and does "
                    ."not order by roles.name ASC then roles.id ASC.\n"
                    .$this->census->methodSource($unit);
            }
        }

        $this->assertSame(
            [],
            $offenders,
            "The lane's order is roles.name ASC then roles.id ASC, and a deterministic order is only a deadlock cure "
            ."when every participant uses the SAME one:\n  ".implode("\n  ", $offenders),
        );
    }
}
~~~

> **The census is exact, not approximate — that is what the AST buys.** Run at `dev` `630afa86f`, the walk above returns **twenty** writer methods and **no** false positives: no comment, no docblock, no unrelated `Product::firstOrCreate`. Rev 1's own note — *"`enumerateWriters()` will report files this plan has not classified… resolve it by narrowing the enumeration honestly"* — is **withdrawn**, because there is nothing to narrow and therefore nothing an agent under time pressure could quietly narrow away. **If the first run reports an unclassified method, it is a real writer.** Classify it into `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` (and wire the lock) or, only with an owner ruling, into `INITIALIZATION_ONLY`. Record the enumerated set verbatim in the handback.
>
> **After the lane merges, three methods join and none leave:** `LotActionPermissionDelta.php::execute`, `::provisionGeneralManager`, `::synchronizeSeededRole` — already in the fixtures above. Phase 0 re-derives their lines; the fixtures are keyed by **method name**, not by line, so a lane rebase cannot invalidate them.
>
> **What `every_multi_row_roles_lock_uses_the_lane_row_order` finds on the merged tree, measured — this is the assertion rev 2 shipped RED** (plan gate r2 **B2-2**). Every `lockForUpdate()` whose chain root is `Role` at `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `52f5ad796`:
>
> | Chain | Predicate on `roles` | Verdict |
> |---|---|---|
> | `LotActionPermissionDelta::resolveCatalogueRoles` `:114-116` | `whereIn('name', $names)` | **multi-row** → asserted → carries `orderBy('name')->orderBy('id')` ✅ |
> | `LotActionPermissionDelta::provisionGeneralManager` `:142-143` | `where('name', GeneralManager)` | single-row → **not asserted** |
> | `RoleController::assignRole` `:379` | `where('name', $validated['role'])` | single-row → **not asserted** |
> | `UserController::store` `:266` | `where('name', $validated['role'])` | single-row → **not asserted** |
> | `UserController::update` `:388` | `whereIn('name', $roles)` | **multi-row** → asserted → carries `orderBy('name')->orderBy('id')` ✅ |
> | `EnsurePermissionsRunner::resolveCatalogueRoles` (this wave) | `whereIn('name', $names)` | **multi-row** → asserted → carries the order ✅ |
>
> The three non-`Role` locks in those same methods — `User::query()…lockForUpdate()` at `RoleController.php:374` and `UserController.php:264,383`, and `UserCompanyMembership::query()…orderBy('company_id')->lockForUpdate()` at `RoleController.php:376` and `UserController.php:265,385` — are **not roles queries** and the rule does not speak about them. Rev 2's per-method `assertGreaterThanOrEqual(2, count($orderBys))` counted `orderBy('company_id')` as one of its two and would have failed `assignRole` on a query about a different table entirely.
>
>
> **The twenty-three methods gate r3 reported as offenders, and what rev 4's rule does with each — plan gate r3 BLOCKER `B3-1`.** The gate executed rev 3's own extracted support class over `app` + `database` and got twenty-three `rootKnown = false` records, on `dev` `630afa86f` **and** on `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `52f5ad796`. Every one was re-read at the lane tip for this revision; the root shape is the chain's own text, and the **lexical** column is a `\broles\b|\bRole\b` scan of the AST-bounded method source — the exact test `methodMentionsRoles()` performs.

> **Ten are dismissed with a proof** (`NOT_ROLES` — the root resolves to a named non-role model or a non-`roles` table literal) and **thirteen are dismissed because they never write the word `roles`** (`UNRESOLVED` + no lexical marker). **None of the twenty-three is silently skipped:** each falls into a rule stated above the visitor, and if any of them ever *did* mention `roles`, it would come back as an `unresolved_role_candidate` and fail the test by name.
>
> **The expected result on the post-lane tree, stated so a green run can be checked rather than trusted:** `every_multi_row_roles_lock_uses_the_lane_row_order` produces **zero offenders**. It examines exactly the six chains in the table above — five at the lane tip plus `EnsurePermissionsRunner::resolveCatalogueRoles`, which this wave adds — of which three are multi-row and all three carry `orderBy('name')` then `orderBy('id')`, both ascending; three are the documented single-row equality exception. Every other `lockForUpdate()` in `app` + `database` classifies as `NOT_ROLES` or as an unresolved chain in a method with no `roles`/`Role` token, and is not examined.
>
> **Re-run the census before trusting this table.** It is measured at `52f5ad796`, and both lanes are still open. Phase 0 re-derives the tips; if the count is no longer twenty-three, or a NEW method appears carrying both an unresolvable root and the word `roles`, that is a **finding** — report it, do not widen `ROLE_LEXICAL_MARKERS` or add a skip list to make it go away. The one legitimate repair is to make the chain decidable at the call site.
> **The whole rule, in one sentence, so a reviewer can check it without reading the visitor:** *a `roles` `FOR UPDATE` that can return more than one row must order by `roles.name` then `roles.id`; a one-row one has nothing to order and is not asked to.*

      3. `EnsurePermissionsCustomisationGuardTest`'s missing-admin case fails on `assertSame(1, $exitCode)` with an observed `0`, before the `AdminRoleMissing` throw replaces rev 1's `$missingRoles[] = 'admin'`. **That zero is the defect**: it is the exit code a deploy checklist would have written `permissions_ensure=ok` on, for a tenant where every gated route 403s every caller.

      **A guard that was never observed failing is not a guard.**

      ```
      Phase 0.2.4: Add permissions:ensure(-fleet) and wire the write lock into every runtime role-grant writer

      One commit by spec ruling (§8, 0b-14). The 0b-window argument is the reason:
      permissions:ensure's only guard is an equality check, wave 1's customised_at
      column does not exist yet, and an equality check that is not serialized
      against the operator edit path is a race with no detector. Wiring the ten
      runtime writer entry points and shipping the command together means every
      interleaving is
      one of two — the operator commits before ensure takes the lock (the post-lock
      re-read sees the change, matchesPreWave0b is false, the role falls into
      admin_only=), or after it releases (the edit lands on top of a grant set ensure
      had already approved). There is no third, because no unlocked writer is left.

      ensure is create-only, grants admin unconditionally, grants a --grant-to role
      only when matchesPreWave0b() is true from the POST-LOCK read, snapshots
      eligibility ONCE per role and writes the approved additions as one batch,
      writes no marker, and takes the lane's team boundary and NULL-or-tenant role
      predicate verbatim — after the lane merges the legacy roles are NULL-team while
      general_manager is tenant-team-scoped, so a default-context lookup would lock
      one topology and write another.

      ResetTenantCommand moves from INITIALIZATION-ONLY to LOCKED (spec 4.3.2's
      rev-8 M-2 ruling): it refuses only in production, --force skips the prompt, and
      it recreates a live schema with no maintenance mode, so "no operator can be
      concurrent" was false for that row. The DROP SCHEMA itself stays out of scope,
      by name.

      A tenant with no resolvable `admin` is FAILED with reason=admin_role_missing
      and exits non-zero on the APPLYING and the DRY-RUN path. Its routes are gated
      on keys nobody holds, Spatie fails closed, and every caller including the
      operator gets 403 — so a run that cannot reach admin must never let a deploy
      checklist record permissions_ensure=ok.

      PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest is a CALL-SITE census over nikic/php-parser,
      not a file-substring grep: it attributes every Spatie write to the METHOD that
      contains it and asserts that method acquires BEFORE its first write. A new
      unlocked method in a file that mentions the lock elsewhere now fails, and a
      docblock can no longer satisfy the proof. Private helpers that write under a
      caller's lock are declared in LOCK_INHERITED_FROM and PROVEN — the entry point
      acquires before calling them, and nothing else calls them.

      Spec 4.3.5, 4.3.2. EC-20b, EC-20c. Plan gate r1 B0b-3, B0b-5, M0b-1, M0b-3.

      Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
      Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Bp1WuD39YqaMWiHR8vPDNN
      ```
