## Reviewer gate

**`tenancy-authz-reviewer` — mandatory, on the whole diff.** Prompt outline:

> Review lane `lane/rbac-w0b` (base `dev` at `<sha>`, tip `<sha>`) against
> `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` rev 9
> §§4.3.2, 4.3.3, 4.3.5, 4.4.3, 4.4.4, 4.9.3, 4.9.4, 6 and 8's wave-0b rows, and
> against this plan. Cite `file:line` for every claim; verify against the tree,
> never from the diff alone.
>
> 1. **Is the lock ONE domain?** Confirm `PermissionWriteLock::key()` produces the
>    same string and the same hash function as `LotActionPermissionDelta`'s own
>    advisory lock. A different key is not a weaker lock — it is **no** lock
>    between the two writers. Then confirm every **multi-row** `roles`
>    `FOR UPDATE` orders by `roles.name` then `roles.id` — and confirm the
>    narrowing is honest: list every `lockForUpdate()` in the diff whose chain
>    root is `Role`, say for each whether an equality on `roles.name`/`roles.id`
>    limits it to one row, and say which pair of roles would deadlock if a
>    multi-row one used `id` alone. **A single-row lock is exempt because it has
>    nothing to order, not because ordering it was inconvenient**; if any
>    single-row claim is wrong, that is a blocker.
>  2. **Is the writer partition real, at METHOD level?** Re-run
>    `PermissionWriterCensus` yourself and print the full `<path>::<method>` set.
>    Name every method it enumerates that the three fixtures do not classify.
>    Then check the two exemption lists specifically: for each
>    `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM` entry, confirm from the AST that its declared entry
>    point acquires before calling it **and** that nothing else in the file calls
>    it; for each `INITIALIZATION_ONLY` entry, confirm it is genuinely
>    unreachable from an authenticated request. **A file-level answer is not an
>    answer** — the question is whether the METHOD acquires before its first
>    write. Add an unlocked method to `RoleController` and confirm the test goes
>    red naming it.
> 3. **Is `ensure`'s eligibility check decisive?** Trace the four statements
>    between `acquire()` and the first `givePermissionTo()`. Prove the grant set
>    it compares was read **after** the lock, and that the snapshot is taken once
>    per role and not once per key. Then construct the interleaving that would
>    lose an operator edit and show why it cannot occur.
> 4. **Does `ensure` resolve a tenant-team `general_manager` from the console?**
>    Open the resolution query. Confirm the NULL-or-tenant predicate, that
>    `tenant_id` is preserved as found, that no role is re-homed, and that the
>    registrar's team id is restored on the failure path as well as the success
>    path.
> 5. **Is every gated key actually seeded?** For each of the nineteen, find it in
>    `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::legacyPermissionNames()` and cite the line. Then
>    name, per key, which seeded roles hold it and which live UI surface calls the
>    route — an undeclared or unheld key is a silent 403 for everyone including
>    `admin`, which is the exact defect 0b-1 exists to fix.
> 6. **Is any live surface broken?** Enumerate every frontend and POS caller of
>    the forty-one gated routes and, for each, name the roles that can reach the
>    calling page and whether they hold the key. `services.*` is the known one —
>    verify the FE guard moved to `services.update` and that no second `services.edit`
>    reference survives.
> 7. **Is the ceiling arithmetic right?** Re-derive 127/126 independently from the
>    live router. Confirm no baseline entry is left stale and that the anti-growth
>    direction is now fail-closed rather than skipped.
> 8. **`BatchActionAccess`:** confirm the `can:` was added **beside** it, that the
>    classifier still refuses to count it as a gate, and that the flag's default
>    is unchanged.
> 9. **`ResetTenantCommand`:** confirm the lock is taken **after** the schema
>    recreate, that the existing `setPermissionsTeamId` at its original position
>    survives, and that the plan's out-of-scope statement about `DROP SCHEMA` is
>    still true of the code.
> 10. **Overlap:** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` and `lane/t2-receipt-spine` are both
>     **entry conditions** and are therefore already ancestors of `dev` — confirm
>     that with `git merge-base --is-ancestor` for each, and confirm the seven
>     T2-owned paths in Phase 0 were edited on top of the merged state, not
>     around it. Then re-run `git diff --name-only dev...<lane> -- <path>` for
>     every path in the diff against **any lane opened since** the entry
>     condition was checked. Any hit is a blocker.
> 10b. **0b-15 — do the gates and the guards actually match?** For each of the four gated
>    reads, name every `apps/web` caller and the roles that can reach its page, and confirm
>    the page's guard uses the SAME key the route does. `RolesPage` (both queries), the
>    `/settings/roles` route, the Settings card and `UsersPage`'s roles query are the four
>    surfaces; a fifth caller anywhere in `apps/web/src` or `apps/pos/src` is a blocker.
>    Then confirm the Playwright spec asserts the **absence** of the requests, not a 403 —
>    a page that fires a request it knows will be refused is the defect this task exists to
>    close, and a 403 in the network log means the guard is not doing its job.
> 11. **The four open questions Q-0b-1..Q-0b-4:** answer each, or say which need
>     the owner. Do not let them merge as discovered decisions.
>
> Verdict: MERGE / MERGE-WITH-FIXES / CHANGES-REQUIRED. Never auto-merge.

**`inventory-costing-reviewer` — mandatory**, for 0b-5 (a batch delete and a batch recall) and 0b-13 (a counting-item submit). Ask specifically: does gating the batch writes on permission change *when* stock moves, or only *who* may move it? Does `can:inventory.adjust` on the counting-item submit match the authority the rest of the counting flow assumes, or is `inventory.adjustments.create` the truer key? Is the filed location-scope gap (`docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-batch-writes-ignore-location-scope.md`) an accurate description of what the controller does?

**`frontend-conventions-reviewer` — mandatory, and a PRECONDITION for Task 6's and Task 13b's commits, not a review of them afterwards** (owner's standing rule: every FE milestone). The lane touches the **eighty-seven** `apps/web` call sites the Task 6 census script prints, six service-category call sites, `apps/web/src/routes/index.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/organisms/Sidebar/Sidebar.tsx`, `apps/web/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts`, `permissionsMap.generated.ts` (regenerated **twice**, in Task 6 and again in Task 14), three locale bundles and two new Playwright specs. Ask specifically:
> 1. Is the `services.edit` → `services.update` guard change complete — no second `services.edit` reference anywhere in `apps/web` or `apps/pos`?
> 2. **Is every one of the EIGHTY-SEVEN rows in Task 6's census actually guarded** — re-run `scripts/census_0b.py` yourself against the merged tree, confirm no file prints `UNASSIGNED`, confirm table C is still a single entry (`DocumentForm.tsx:391`, and say what you found when you read it), and **diff your output against the table pasted in the plan** — and does each guard use the SAME key as the route it calls? A guard on a different key is worse than none, because it looks addressed. Pay particular attention to rows 17–22, the `service-categories.*` half rev 2 omitted — the categories **route**, the list page's **query**, its **create** and **update/delete** controls, and the **two other pages** (`ServiceListPage`, `ServiceForm`) that read the same endpoint and that no route guard protects — and to rows 23–27, the nav and link guards rev 3 asserted in its e2e and forbade in its steps: are the three new `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` rows **self-mapped** (not widened to a role list), and does each nav child carry the key its own route now checks?
> 2b. **`AddCompanyModal`:** is the guard on the **component** (an early `return null` plus a disabled submit), and is the *no production instantiation* fact recorded rather than a trigger invented? Re-run the grep yourself: `grep -rn 'AddCompanyModal' apps/web/src` should show the definition, three barrels/shims and two `__tests__` renderings, and no other JSX use.
> 2c. **`useCategoryTree()`:** is the gate at the **two callers** (`CategoriesPage.tsx:27`, `CategoryForm.tsx:26`) rather than inside the hook, and was the **namesake** hook at `features/catalog/api/queries.ts:76` left alone with its three callers untouched?
> 2d. **`RequirePermission`:** does any new in-page control guard use the component **without** `fallback={null}`? Without it the component *navigates to `/dashboard`* instead of hiding a control (`features/auth/components/RequirePermission.tsx:65-80`). One such guard is a blocker, because it turns a hidden button into a page the user cannot stay on.
> 3. Are **all six** `/services/categories` API call sites fixed — including `ServiceListPage.tsx:49`, which the gate register did not name — with `api.put` → `api.patch` on update, while the two SPA paths (`ServiceListPage.tsx:117`, `Sidebar.tsx:148`) and every `tenantScopedKey(['service-categories'])` are untouched?
> 4. Do the query keys still satisfy `audit-tanstack-keys.mjs`, and does `audit-design-system.mjs` stay green (the guards should add no markup and no colour literal)?
> 5. Does any new guard introduce a hand-rolled type beside a generated DTO, or a user-facing string outside `t()`?
> 6. Is the regenerated map byte-identical across two generations? Are the new label keys present in **all three** locales with real strings, or is French/Arabic owed and recorded as R-0b-1?
> 7. In both Playwright specs, does the manager arm assert the **absence** of the request rather than a 403? A 403 in the network log means the guard is not doing its job. And does it assert the **redirect to `/dashboard`** rather than an `errors.permissionDenied` surface the router does not render off `/dashboard`?
> 8. Is `permissionsMap.generated.ts` staged in **Task 6** (not only Task 14), and does `pnpm typecheck` pass at Task 6's commit **in isolation**? Every new `permission="…"` and `hasPermission('…')` in that commit is a type error without it.

No treasury or fiscal reviewer: this wave touches neither module.

`docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w0b-2026-09-10.md` — contents enumerated in Task 14 step 8.

### Residuals to record in it

| # | Residual | Owner / wave |
|---|---|---|
| **R-0b-1** | The French and Arabic labels for the new modules and the `operate` action, if they shipped as English placeholders. A missing label degrades to the raw key in the matrix; an English placeholder in the Arabic bundle is worse, because it looks translated. | translator / wave 1's `permissions:export-label-skeleton` |
| **R-0b-2** | `permissions:ensure`, `permissions:ensure-fleet`, `EnsurePermissionsRunner`, `DryRunRollback`, **`AdminRoleMissing`**, `EnsurePermissionsResult`, `EnsurePermissionsOutcome` and **`scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh`** are **explicitly a pre-wave-1 stopgap**. `permissions:sync` supersedes them entirely — step 2 creates every registry key and step 6 applies template grants, a strict superset. ## Deploy

Spec §8's wave-0b runbook, with Q-0b-4's two invocations. **`tenants:seed RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` is not in this sequence at all** — on a marked tenant with `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE=false` it takes the preservation branch and writes nothing, so the 0b keys would never reach a tenant whose routes are already gated on them: 403 for everyone including `admin`.

### The two invocations are ONE shell wrapper, and the wrapper is what writes the status file (plan gate r1 **BLOCKER B0b-6**)

Rev 1 listed the two commands and then a step *"write the status file, on both arms… `=ok` on success"* — with no executable definition of *success*. A first invocation that **fails** followed by a second that **succeeds** would have written `permissions_ensure=ok` over a fleet that never received twelve of the nineteen keys. Rev 1's Q-0b-1 also claimed `permissions:ensure-fleet` *writes* the status file; **it does not** — the command renders per-tenant and aggregate markers and returns an exit code, and nothing in `EnsurePermissionsFleet::handle()` touches a file. **Rev 2 corrects the claim and makes success atomic.**

Create `scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh` (**a wave-0b deliverable, committed in Task 14, deleted by wave 1 with the commands it wraps**):

~~~bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Wave 0b permission deploy. TWO invocations, ONE success condition.
#
# The status file is written HERE, not by the command: `permissions:ensure-fleet`
# renders markers and returns an exit code, and no single exit code can express
# "both halves of a two-invocation deploy succeeded". Writing it from inside
# either invocation would let a failed first run be overwritten by a successful
# second one — a fleet missing twelve of the nineteen keys, recorded as healthy.
#
# Deleted in wave 1, in the same commit that deletes permissions:ensure(-fleet).
set -euo pipefail

STATUS_FILE="${PERMISSIONS_ENSURE_STATUS_FILE:-/var/run/autoerp/permissions-ensure.status}"
ARTISAN="${ARTISAN:-php artisan}"

mkdir -p "$(dirname "$STATUS_FILE")"

# Every marker is written to a temp file in the SAME directory and then renamed
# over the target. rename(2) within one filesystem is atomic, so a reader never
# sees a half-written marker and a `kill -9` between the two lines leaves the
# PREVIOUS marker intact rather than a truncated one (plan gate r2 minor 5).
write_status() {
  local tmp
  tmp="$(mktemp "${STATUS_FILE}.XXXXXX")"
  printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$tmp"
  mv -f "$tmp" "$STATUS_FILE"
}

# CLEAR THE PREVIOUS RESULT BEFORE DOING ANY WORK. Without this, a deploy that is
# killed mid-run — OOM, a cancelled pipeline, a `kill -9` — leaves whatever the
# LAST deploy wrote, and a stale `permissions_ensure=ok` is exactly the reading
# that lets a fleet missing twelve keys be recorded as healthy. `pending` is
# neither ok nor failed, and the checklist row treats it as NOT DONE.
write_status "permissions_ensure=pending"

fail() {
  write_status "permissions_ensure=failed:$1"
  echo "PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-DEPLOY FAILED at $1 — status written to $STATUS_FILE" >&2
  exit 1
}

# Invocation 1 — the twelve admin-only keys. No --grant-to: a flat --grant-to
# list would give a manager twelve keys the spec makes admin-only.
$ARTISAN permissions:ensure-fleet \
  --keys=channels.view --keys=channels.create --keys=channels.update --keys=channels.operate \
  --keys=categories.view --keys=categories.create --keys=categories.update --keys=categories.delete \
  --keys=progression.view --keys=progression.manage \
  --keys=purchase-hub.orders.create --keys=companies.create \
  || fail "invocation_1_admin_only_keys"

# Invocation 2 — the seven template keys, all in ONE run, because the
# eligibility snapshot is taken once per role before any of that role's
# additions are written.
$ARTISAN permissions:ensure-fleet \
  --keys=credit-notes.cancel \
  --keys=services.view --keys=services.create --keys=services.update \
  --keys=service-categories.view --keys=service-categories.create --keys=service-categories.update \
  --grant-to=manager --grant-to=general_manager \
  || fail "invocation_2_template_keys"

write_status "permissions_ensure=ok"
echo "PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-DEPLOY OK — both invocations exited 0; status written to $STATUS_FILE"
~~~

`set -euo pipefail` plus the explicit `|| fail` on each invocation means **`ok` is written only when both exited 0**, and any other outcome leaves `failed:<which>` naming the half that broke — or, if the script never got to say anything at all, `pending`. **Three states, not two** (plan gate r2 **minor 5**): the `pending` marker written before invocation 1 is what makes an abruptly killed deploy distinguishable from a healthy one, and the temp-file-plus-`mv` in `write_status` is what makes a marker whole or absent rather than truncated. A checklist row that reads `pending` treats the deploy as **not done**, exactly as it treats `failed:`. Add `--dry-run` to both invocations via `ARTISAN` or a `--dry-run` pass-through when rehearsing (Task 14 Step 6 runs them directly, not through the wrapper, because a dry run must not write a status file at all).

| # | Step | Notes |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | `bash scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh` | **one command.** It runs both `permissions:ensure-fleet` invocations under `set -euo pipefail`, and writes `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-ensure.status` — `permissions_ensure=ok` **only if both exited 0**, otherwise `permissions_ensure=failed:<which>`. Additive, marker-safe, idempotent. |
| 2 | `php artisan permission:cache-reset` | already wired at boot (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:190-191`), run explicitly here because the deploy wrote grants out of band. |
| 3 | `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map` | the FE map's input grew by nineteen keys. |

**Health check (the wave-0b deploy-checklist row).** *After the 0b deploy, confirm `scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh` exited **zero** and that `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-ensure.status` reads `permissions_ensure=ok`. A `permissions_ensure=failed:<which>` names which of the two invocations broke; a `permissions_ensure=pending` means the script was killed before it could classify itself — treat it exactly as a failure, and re-run. If not, grep the container log for the `PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-FLEET` aggregate line — it names every failed or blocked tenant id with its reason — resolve the cause (a `tenant_not_ready` tenant needs its rolling migration to succeed first) and re-run `php artisan permissions:ensure-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug> --keys=… --grant-to=…` for those tenants, **each selector resolving to exactly one tenant** per §4.3.3's step-1a contract, before considering the deploy complete.*

**Verification.** The per-tenant `PERMISSIONS-ENSURE` marker shows non-zero `created`/`granted` on the first run and `created=0 granted=0` on a re-run (EC-32c). Every tenant named in `admin_only=` goes to residual **R-0b-7**.

**Rollback point.** Revert the wave's commits. The added `permissions` rows are **inert** once no route references them; `permissions:prune-orphans` is **not** run as part of a rollback (it does not exist until wave 3, and it is a deliberate operator action even then).

---

## Rollback

Every task is one self-contained, path-scoped commit. They are independent in the reverse direction, with three ordering constraints:

| Revert | Effect | Must also revert |
|---|---|---|
| Task 14 (handback) | documentation only | — |
| Task 13 (convention-09 tests) | three tests disappear | — |
| Task 12 (CI) | the ratchet goes dark in CI again and anti-growth returns to skipped | — |
| Task 11 (glossary) | six rows disappear; the lane's General-manager row is untouched either way | — |
| Task 10 (`PermissionSeeder`) | the colliding seeder and its seven test references return | — |
| Task 9 (`RequireAnyPermission`) | the 403 body loses `details`; T2's generic message survives | — |
| Task 8 (counting-item gate) | that write becomes ungated | its baseline entry and ceiling revert in the same commit |
| Task 7 (batch gates) | the two batch writes fall back to the flag-conditional middleware, i.e. **ungated** by default | same |
| Task 6 (eighteen keys + gates) | forty-one routes become ungated; the keys leave the catalogue | Task 13 first (its tests use the keys). **The already-deployed `permissions` rows survive on tenants and are inert.** |
| Task 5 (`credit-notes.cancel`) | the key leaves the catalogue and that route 403s everyone again | Task 6 first |
| Task 4 (ensure + wiring) | the two commands disappear and the eight writers lose the lock | Tasks 5–8 remain valid but **undeployable** — nothing can reach a marked tenant. Revert 6 and 5 too, or the routes are gated on keys no tenant can receive. |
| Task 3 (`TenantFleetRunner`) | the fleet iterator disappears | Task 4 first |
| Task 2 (`PermissionWriteLock`) | the lock helper disappears | Tasks 4 and 3 first |
| Task 1 (baseline + rename map) | the oracles disappear | Task 4 first |

**Two migrations? No — this wave adds none, changes no column and creates no table.** A full revert of the lane returns the tree to its pre-wave state; what it cannot revert is the `permissions` rows and `role_has_permissions` pivots a deploy already wrote. Those are **additive and inert** once no route references them, which is exactly why the deploy is safe to run before the code is certain.

Output: **87 rows across 17 files** (46 API + 41 navigation), each with its required key **and the guard Task 6 applies**, plus a `GUARDS` self-check that prints `UNASSIGNED` for any unguarded file and a table C for unresolvable path arguments (one entry: `DocumentForm.tsx:391`). The rev-4 row numbers are preserved as a cross-reference table. New guards: `ServicePicker` + both mount sites, `ServiceDetailPage`'s query/link/DELETE, both "Add Service" links, the six `/categories` transitive callers, `CommandPalette:73`, `InventoryHubPage:41-46`. Thirteen files join Step 9's `git add`; four join File Structure. |

Every service edit in the shipped app is a 405. | **Step 3(d) gains the service update**: `api.put` → `api.patch` at `ServiceForm.tsx:142`. Two consequences land in the same commit: the neighbouring spec's `api` double (`__tests__/tenantScope.test.tsx:24-31`) gains a `patch` member and a hoisted `mockApiPatch`, and the new Vitest file 6 asserts `mockApiPatch` was called and `mockApiPut` was **not**. The e2e manager arm now asserts **`PATCH /api/v1/services/{id}` 200**, by verb, instead of the word "succeed". |

Three further files are named for the rev-5 rows (`ServiceDetailPage`, `ServicePicker`, `CategorySelector`) — **nine pairs across nine files**. **Task 13 supplies the full trait plus three complete classes**, every `actingAs()` carrying `->withHeader('X-Company-Id', …)`; the two inventory-shaped helpers are the one thing deliberately left to be read off the sibling suites, with the reason stated. |

The body was right; the prose was not. | The description is corrected to match the body — a mutable `private` property resolved once from the container in `setUp()`, **never** the `app()` helper — and says why, so nobody "fixes" the body to match the old prose. |

### Two facts rev 5 records that gate r4 did not raise

1. **`categories.view` is admin-only and has six live callers, not two.** Rev 4's ruling **(B)** gated `useCategoryTree()` at `CategoriesPage` and `CategoryForm` and deliberately left the `features/catalog` namesake alone, reasoning that gating it would put a `categories.view` check on a product-form dropdown *by accident*. The census shows that check is not caused by gating — it is caused by the **backend gate this wave adds**, and rev 4 shipped four forms that would have 403'd: the product form, the coupon form, the promotion form and create-counting. All six callers are guarded; the UX consequence (an empty, disabled picker for a non-admin) is **residual R-0b-10**, owner ruling owed pre-deploy. `features/catalog/pages/CategoryManagementPage.tsx` has no route at all — **residual R-0b-11**.
2. **The lane advanced after the gate's pin, harmlessly.** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` is now `a7010fe4d`, one commit past `52f5ad796`: *"Phase 1.2.10: Record the transfer scope ruling and verification"*, three documentation files, +55/−11, **no `apps/` path**. Every `lane/w-lot-a-1a:<path>:<line>` citation in this plan still holds byte-for-byte at the tip. The pin stays at `52f5ad796` because that is the tree the citations were measured against; the fact is recorded in the pin table so the next reader does not re-measure to find out.

### Findings NOT applied

None. Gate r4 raised seven findings and rev 5 applies all seven.

## Change log rev 3 → rev 4

Register: `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-plan-0b-codex-gate-r3.md` (**CHANGES-REQUIRED**, 1 BLOCKER / 5 MAJOR / 4 minor + a citation audit). Prompt: `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-plan-gate-RBAC-0b-round-3-2026-09-10.md`. Every ID is mapped; `[R]` marks a finding whose premise was re-verified against code before it was applied, and `[P]` one applied only in part, with the rejected half evidenced.

| Gate ID | Verified how | What rev 4 changed |
|---|---|---|
| **B3-1** — the supplied row-order census cannot be green on the post-lane tree; twenty-three false offenders | **[R] Confirmed, and re-measured.** All twenty-three were re-read at `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `52f5ad796`: every one is a `lockForUpdate()` whose chain root is not a static call, and **none** contains the token `roles` or `Role` anywhere in its AST-bounded method source. Ten resolve to a provably non-role root (`StockLevel::query()`, `Voucher::query()`, `Document::query()`, `HeldOrder::where()`, `Product::query()`, `BankStatementLine::query()`, `$this->db->table('pos_terminals')`); thirteen are genuinely unresolvable (`$template->accounts()`, `$this->rowQuery(…)`, `$payment->instrument()`) | **`recordRoleLock()` is rewritten around a three-valued root classifier.** `ROLE_QUERY_ROOTS = ['Role']` (a last-name match) becomes `ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES` — **fully qualified**, resolved through the file's `use` map — plus `ROLE_TABLE` and `ROLE_LEXICAL_MARKERS`. A root is `ROLES` when it is `Role::…` resolving to a listed class, `DB::table('roles')`, `$anything->table('roles')`, `$this-><relation>()` whose same-class relation names a role class, or **a variable assigned from any of those in the same method**; `NOT_ROLES` when it resolves to something else (**ignored**); `UNRESOLVED` otherwise, and an `UNRESOLVED` chain is refused as an `unresolved_role_candidate` **only if its method mentions `roles`/`Role`**. The visitor gains a `use` map, a same-method assignment map, a class-method source map and the `$source` string; `PermissionWriterVisitor::__construct` takes a second parameter; `NodeFinder` joins the imports. `rootKnown` is replaced by `verdict` in all three shape docblocks and in the assertion. Two further corrections the gate named: **`whereIn` is monotonic** (`multiRow = $sawWhereIn \|\| ! $sawEquality`, order-independent — rev 3 let a later equality demote it) and **ascending order is asserted** (`isAscending()`: one-arg `orderBy` or a literal `'asc'`; `orderBy('name','desc')` and `orderByDesc` no longer satisfy the rule). A **twenty-three-row disposition table** sits under the test with each root shape, verdict, lexical result and reason, followed by the **expected green**: six chains examined, three multi-row and all three ordered, zero offenders |
| **M3-1** — e2e-visible navigation and control hiding asserted but not implemented | **[P] Confirmed for four of the six surfaces; two rejected with evidence.** `Sidebar.tsx:148` serviceCategories carries `permission: 'services'`; `:217` categories, `:276` channels and `:277` channelOrders carry no permission of their own; `ServiceListPage.tsx:116-121`'s link is unconditional. **But `grep -n growth` over `Sidebar.tsx` returns nothing and no file in `apps/web/src` links to `/growth`**, and there is no company-create nav entry either — the only company-create affordance is `CompanySelector.tsx:113-120`, already census row 14 | **Census rows 23–27 added; ruling (D) written; Step 3(g) added.** Four `Sidebar.tsx` nav children gain a `permission:`, and `ServiceListPage`'s link is wrapped. **A consequence the gate did not name and rev 4 resolves rather than defers:** the nav `permission` field is typed `ModuleKey = keyof typeof MODULE_PERMISSIONS` (`Sidebar.tsx:110,120`, `usePermissions.ts:182`), so the three keys need **self-mapped `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` rows** — `usePermissions.ts` is therefore edited, and rev 3's "expected UNTOUCHED" note is replaced by the finding it invited. Both files join Task 6's `git add`; `Sidebar.tsx` and `usePermissions.ts` gain File-Structure rows; the caller count moves 22 → 27 in all six places it appears. The Growth and company-create nav assertions are **corrected in the e2e**, not implemented: the redirect and request-absence assertions stay, the no-nav facts are recorded, and the spec is explicitly forbidden to add a nav entry in order to hide it |

### Findings NOT applied, with the reason

| Gate ID | Why not |

| **M3-1**, the Growth-navigation half | There is no Growth nav entry to hide. `grep -n "growth"` over `apps/web/src/components/organisms/Sidebar/Sidebar.tsx` at `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `52f5ad796` returns nothing, and no file in `apps/web/src` links to `/growth`: the two Progression routes at `routes/index.tsx:3210-3227` are reachable by deep link only. Implementing the assertion would mean **adding** a nav entry in order to gate it — a surface invented to be tested, which proves nothing about the shipped app and violates the same rule the plan already applies to `AddCompanyModal`. The e2e keeps the Growth deep-link redirect and request-absence assertions and records the no-nav fact; the same reasoning covers "company-creation navigation", which is `CompanySelector`'s button (census row 14, already guarded) and not a nav entry |
| **M0b-2**, the ordering half *(carried from rev 3, unchanged)* | Applied as far as it goes (every red is now named, supplied as a body, and reproducible), but **not** by re-sequencing Tasks 1–4 so that every test is authored before its implementation. Doing so would mean interleaving the supplied class bodies with their test bodies at a per-method granularity, which makes the plan unreadable as a dispatch document and does not change what an executing agent must produce. The critique stays in the handback as an execution-discipline note: **write each test file before the implementation it covers**, and if a red can only be produced by removing a branch, say so in the evidence |

### Two defects rev 4 fixes that gate r3 did not raise

1. **`RefundController::cancelCreditNote` requires a `reason`.** Rev 3's Task 5 posted no body, so the holder case would have returned **422** — indistinguishable at a glance from the authorization failure the test exists to detect. Both arms now send it. `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/Document/Presentation/Controllers/RefundController.php:233-235`.
2. **The Sidebar nav `permission` field is a `ModuleKey`, not a `Permission`.** Implementing M3-1 as the gate described it — `permission: 'channels.view'` on a nav child — is a **TypeScript error** until `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` carries the key, because `UI-01` closed that key space deliberately (`usePermissions.ts:55-63,174-182`). Rev 4 supplies the three self-mapped rows, and Step 3(g) states the compile error as that sub-step's expected red so an executing agent does not "fix" it by widening `ModuleKey` or casting.

### Two findings rev 3 adds that gate r2 did not raise

Both were found while applying M2-3, both are blocker-class for an executing agent, and both are recorded here rather than folded silently into a task.

| # | Finding | Evidence | Applied at |
|---|---|---|---|
| **N-1** | **Task 6 cannot pass `pnpm typecheck` as rev 2 sequenced it.** `usePermissions.ts:8` types `Permission = GeneratedPermission \| UiAliasPermission`. At `lane/w-lot-a-1a`, `permissionsMap.generated.ts` contains **no** `services.*`, `service-categories.*`, `channels.*`, `categories.*`, `progression.*` or `companies.*` key (`roles.view:244` and `users.assign-roles:274` are the only two this wave's vocabulary overlaps), and `uiAliasPermissions.ts:2-13` carries only `services.view/create/edit` **and forbids additions in its own first line**. So every new `permission="…"` and `hasPermission('…')` in Task 6 is a **type error** until the map is regenerated — which rev 2 deferred to Task 14 Step 3, eight commits later, while its own Global Constraint says *no commit in this wave is knowingly red* | `git show lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/web/src/hooks/permissionsMap.generated.ts \| grep "'services\.\|'channels\.\|'categories\.\|'progression\.\|'companies\."` → **empty**; `usePermissions.ts:1-8,90`; `uiAliasPermissions.ts:1-15` | New **Step 3(f)** in Task 6: regenerate `permissionsMap.generated.ts` in that commit, immediately after Step 1's seeder edit. The file joins Task 6's `git add`. Task 14 Step 3 is unchanged and keeps its determinism gate — it re-runs the same deterministic command twice, so a second regeneration either changes nothing or has found a real defect. The rev-2 sentence asserting the opposite is replaced |
| **N-2** | **`RequirePermission` redirects; it does not render a denial surface.** `if (!hasAccess) { if (fallback) …; if (location.pathname !== '/dashboard') return <Navigate to="/dashboard" …/>; return <PermissionDenied /> }`. Nothing in `apps/web/src` reads `state.permissionDenied`. Two consequences: an in-page control guard **must** pass `fallback={null}` or it navigates the user away instead of hiding a button; and a deep-linked denial lands on **`/dashboard`**, not on `errors.permissionDenied` | `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/web/src/features/auth/components/RequirePermission.tsx:65-80`; `grep -rn 'permissionDenied' apps/web/src` returns only that file; the house precedent for `fallback={null}` is `features/income/pages/IncomeListPage.tsx:49,109` | Ruling **(C)** in Task 6. Every deep-link assertion in **both** Playwright specs, the File Structure rows for both specs, the manual probe B and two verification-checklist rows now assert the redirect and the request absence. The frontend reviewer gate gains a question specifically about a bare `<RequirePermission>` around a control |

### Findings NOT applied, with the reason

| Gate ID | Why not |
|---|---|
| **M0b-2**, the ordering half | Applied as far as it goes (every red is now named and reproducible), but **not** by re-sequencing Tasks 1–4 so that every test is authored before its implementation. Doing so would mean interleaving the supplied class bodies with their test bodies at a per-method granularity, which makes the plan unreadable as a dispatch document and does not change what an executing agent must produce. The critique is recorded in the handback as an execution-discipline note instead: **write each test file before the implementation it covers**, and if a red can only be produced by removing a branch, say so in the evidence. |

### Signature changes for wave 1

**NONE in production; ONE test-fixture contract change** (plan gate r2 **M2-6**; the *"One, and it is deliberate"* phrasing is corrected in rev 4 per plan gate r3 minor 4 — it read as though a shipped API had moved, and none has). The single change is the shape of a **private** constant inside a test class, `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest::LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`, from `array<string, string>` to `array<string, list<string>>`. Everything else wave 1 consumes is unchanged:

| Contract | Status after rev 2 |
|---|---|
| `PermissionWriteLock::acquire(string): void` / `::key(string): string` | **unchanged** |
| `LegacyRoleBaseline::postA1aGrants()` / `::wave0bAdditions(string)` / `::version0(string)` / `::matchesPreWave0b(string, array)` / `::matchesVersion0(string, array)` | **unchanged**. `WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS` gained an `admin` **row**; the constant's shape (`array<string, list<string>>`) and every method signature are identical. Wave 1's adoption arithmetic gets *more correct*, not different — a pristine admin now adopts as pristine, which is what wave 1 already expects |
| `PermissionRenameMap::entries()` / `::sources()` / `::targets()` / `::to(string)` / `::canonicalise(list)` | **unchanged** |
| `TenantFleetRunner::run(array, string, Closure): FleetRunResult`, `FleetTenantOutcome`, `FleetOutcome` | **unchanged** |
| `TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest::fleetCommands()` | **now exists**, which is what wave 1 needs — its plan promises to "extend" a provider rev 1 never created. Wave 1 appends one row: `'permissions:sync-fleet' => ['permissions:sync-fleet', ['--dry-run' => true], 'PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET']` |
| **`PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest::LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`** *(private test-fixture constant, not a production API)* | **CHANGED — `array<string, string>` → `array<string, list<string>>`.** A helper now declares **every** entry point, not one. Wave 1's row becomes `'…/TemplateDeltaApplier.php::applyTo' => ['…/PermissionSyncService.php::sync', '…/RoleController.php::reapplyTemplate']`. See the ruling below. |

#### The `TemplateDeltaApplier` re-pin, stated precisely (plan gate r2 **M2-6**)

Rev 2 said only that wave 1 *"must re-pin to this class name and this three-way split"*. That instruction claimed more than the supplied proof establishes, so rev 3 states the boundary and then picks a branch.

**What the inheritance proof actually covers, exactly.** `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM` proves a helper inherits a caller's lock when, from the AST: (a) **every declared entry point acquires before it calls the helper**, and (b) **the declared list is the complete set of callers in the scanned tree**. In rev 2, (b) was enumerated **only within the helper's own file** — which is a real proof, but only for a **private** helper called from a locked entry in the **same class**, because PHP itself forbids any other caller. That is precisely W-LOT's two: `provisionGeneralManager()` and `synchronizeSeededRole()` are both `private` and both called only from `execute()` (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:45-89,139-180`).

**A separate class gets neither guarantee for free**, so it has exactly two ways in, and wave 1 must choose one rather than inherit an assumption:

1. **`TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo()` acquires the lock itself** — it moves to `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`, and its two callers each acquire again (the advisory lock is transaction-scoped and re-entrant within one transaction, so a second `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the same key is a no-op).
2. **`applyTo()` acquires nothing, and every entry point is enumerated and proven** — it stays in `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`, declaring **all** of its callers, and `PermissionWriterCensus::callersOf()` proves the list is complete **across the whole tree**, not merely across its own file.

> **RULING for wave 1: branch 2.** `TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo()` acquires nothing — its own plan says so (*"It deliberately does **not** take the lock"*, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:81`) — and its callers are proven complete by the census's **cross-file caller enumeration**, which rev 3 adds to `PermissionWriterCensus` as `callersOf()` and which `an_inheriting_helper_declares_every_caller_and_each_one_locks_first` already exercises. Branch 1 would put an acquire inside a class whose whole design premise is that its callers own the transaction.
>
> **One correction to the ruling's premise, recorded rather than silently applied.** The instruction as given said *"`PermissionSyncService::sync()` is its only caller"*. That is **not** what the wave-1 plan says: it gives `applyTo()` **two** callers — *"the ONE implementation of 'apply a template to a role', with two callers"* (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:139,4881`) — and pins them as `[PermissionSyncService::sync, RoleController::reapplyTemplate]` at `:6513`. The *decision* (branch 2, proven by cross-file enumeration) is applied as ruled; the *count* is two, and a fixture that declared one would fail `callersOf()` on the other — which is the mechanism working, one wave early. This is why `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`'s value is a **list**: a scalar could not have expressed the ruling at all.
>
> **What wave 1 must therefore write**, when it lands `PermissionSyncService` and `TemplateDeltaApplier`:
> ```php
> private const LOCK_INHERITED_FROM = [
>     // …the two LotActionPermissionDelta helpers, unchanged…
>     'app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/TemplateDeltaApplier.php::applyTo' => [
>         'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/PermissionSyncService.php::sync',
>         'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::reapplyTemplate',
>     ],
> ];
> ```
> and **both** of those entry points go in `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`, each acquiring before its first call to `applyTo()`. If wave 1 discovers a third caller, the test names it and the fixture grows — it is not a reason to fall back to branch 1 without saying so.

**Three additions wave 1 should consume rather than re-invent**, none of which changes an existing signature:

1. **`EnsurePermissionsResult::markerLine(string $tenantId, bool $write)`** gained its second parameter. This class is **deleted by wave 1** in the same commit that lands `permissions:sync`, so the change reaches wave 1 only as a shape to copy: the sync marker should render `mode=` from its own DTO for the same reason.
2. **`Tests\Architecture\Support\PermissionWriterCensus`** is new and is the class wave 1's M1-3 needs. It now carries three things wave 1 uses directly: `collect()` (the per-method AST census), **`methodSource(array $unit)`** (AST-bounded, so a failure message quotes the right method) and **`callersOf(array $units, string $name)`** (the tree-wide caller enumeration the `TemplateDeltaApplier` re-pin above depends on). Wave 1 adds `PermissionSyncService::sync` and `RoleController::reapplyTemplate` to `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`, and `TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo` to **`LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`** with **both** of them as its entry points. Under rev 1's file-substring test it would have passed on its own disclaiming docblock; under rev 3's it must prove the caller relationship, tree-wide. **Wave 1's plan must re-pin to this class name, this three-way split, and the list-valued `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`.**
3. **`scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh`** joins the list of things wave 1 deletes in the commit that lands `permissions:sync` — alongside `EnsurePermissions`, `EnsurePermissionsFleet`, `EnsurePermissionsRunner`, `DryRunRollback`, **`AdminRoleMissing`**, `EnsurePermissionsResult` and `EnsurePermissionsOutcome`. Residual R-0b-2 is updated to say so.
